# Agricultural Resource Management Using Technologies Like AI, IoT, and Blockchain

  
  
**Ashish Mahalle^([1](#9789815274349-C2.html#aff1), [\*](#9789815274349-C2.html#cor1))****,** **Snehlata Dongre^([1](#9789815274349-C2.html#aff1))**  
¹ Department of Computer Science & Engineering, G. H. Raisoni College of Engineering, Nagpur, India  
  

## Abstract

The future of farming and farmers in India is smart farming, which uses intelligence to integrate information technology and communication tools equipped with sensors and actuators for embedded farm management. This involves using emerging technologies like AI, IoT, and blockchain to employ robots, drones, and artificial intelligence in the agricultural sector, which is modifying traditional farming practices and simultaneously posing a variety of difficulties. The aim is to explore the various tools and equipment used. Pesticides are an essential material used in agricultural land to eliminate insects or other harmful organisms that affect crop yields. However, excessive use of pesticides can result in problems such as decreased soil fertility and an increase in insect species' immunity. To overcome these challenges, a land-specific variable-rate spraying and directional spraying method can be employed, which offers an accurate and flexible alternative strategy. Soil moisture is a crucial parameter in agriculture as it affects plant growth and survival. Various factors like air content, salinity, toxic substances, soil structure, temperature, and heat capacity of the ground can affect soil moisture. Agriculture resource management can be enhanced by designing various technologies like AI, IoT, and blockchain by using IoT sensors, drones and satellites, AI-powered cameras, weather stations, crop yield predictions, disease and pest detection, crop optimization, supply chain transparency, resource optimization, online communities, and data sharing networks. AI optimizes resource allocation and predicts outcomes. IoT provides real-time data for precision farming and livestock monitoring, while blockchain ensures transparency and security in supply chains and transactions, revolutionizing agricultural resource management. Agriculture resource management using technologies like AI, IoT, and blockchain comes with ample potential results like increasing efficiency in agricultural operations, enhancing productivity, improving crop and livestock health, and facilitating knowledge sharing and collaboration.

**Keywords:** Agriculture, Artificial intelligence, Blockchain, Crop monitoring, Crop yeild prediction, Decision support system, Farm automation, IoT (Internet of Things), Machine learning, Resource optimization, Smart farming.  
  

* * *

^(\*) **Corresponding author Ashish Mahalle:** Department of Computer Science & Engineering, G. H. Raisoni College of Engineering, Nagpur, India; Tel: 7276324335; E-mail: [mahalle.ashish04@gmail.com](mailto:mahalle.ashish04@gmail.com)  
  

## INTRODUCTION

Large-scale production of food grains is made possible through sustainable agriculture. Sustainable agriculture encourages farmers to adopt new approaches that help sustain their livelihood and resources. It reduces production costs and focuses on soil quality, thereby minimizing soil degradation. Sustainable agriculture also works on managing water resources and land biodiversity and making the environment more resilient. It is a significant contributor to preserving natural resources, restoring degraded areas, and decreasing the amount of greenhouse gas emissions in agriculture \[[1](#9789815274349-C2.html#r1), [2](#9789815274349-C2.html#r2)\]. In sustainable agriculture, farming is a process that preserves and nurtures nature while also considering the basic needs of future generations and improving farming techniques. Crop rotation, controlling nutrient deficiency, pest and disease control, recycling, and water harvesting are essential basics of smart farming that lead to a safer environment. Biodiversity affects living beings, and waste emissions, pesticides, and degraded dead plants have an adverse effect on the environment. The impact of greenhouse gas emissions on animal life, plants, human beings, and the environment emphasizes the need to create a more conducive atmosphere for all forms of life (Fig. [**1**](#9789815274349-C2.html#f1)).

![](Images/9789815274349-C2-F1.jpg)**Fig. (1))**  
Sustainable agriculture factors. 

Due to soil quality, topography, water resources, climate, and temperature, all available surfaces on the Earth are not suitable for farming, and a homogenous surface is not available throughout \[[3](#9789815274349-C2.html#r3)\]. The total availability of land consistently decreases due to rapid urbanization; thereby, total land utilization for food production also declines (Fig. [**2**](#9789815274349-C2.html#f2)).

![](Images/9789815274349-C2-F2.jpg)**Fig. (2))**  
Challenges in the agriculture sector. 

Every crop field has unique characteristics that are critical for achieving both quality and quantity in a particular crop. These characteristics include soil type, irrigation flow, nutrient presence, and resistance to pests. Spatial and temporal differences are crucial factors in increasing growing crops in identical fields, which is achieved through crop rotation using different seed varieties and modern irrigation techniques. In order to increase yield productivity, farm automation with new technology approaches is needed, such as harvest automation, drones, autonomous tractors, autonomous harvesters, robotic weeders, *etc.* They all have sensors attached to them to gather data within a short span of time. However, the above goal needs to be achieved with minimum ecological impact. The new technologies with sensors, actuators, and communication can help farmers to detect current field operations remotely without a physical presence in the field. Farmers come to know the exact requirements of crops and can take action remotely based on this information \[[4](#9789815274349-C2.html#r4)\]. Wireless sensors are used to monitor the crops' life cycle stages, from crop sowing to crop harvesting. These wireless sensors provide higher efficiency and can be used to detect early-stage issues or infections. From planting to harvesting of crops, farm automation consistently enables the utilization of intelligent tools. AI is powered by complex algorithms, statistical models, and neural networks that enable machines to learn from data and improve their performance over time. Blockchain technology is used to secure and maintain transparency in transactions.

## AGRICULTURAL GROWTH IN INDIA

In ancient agriculture, crop yield was limited to food production on which humans and animals were dependent, and this was known as the conventional agriculture age, where simple tools like khurpi, sickles, and shovels were used for farming, and work was carried out through labor, due to which there was an overall lower percentage of production. In the 19th century, agriculture practices started to change and new technologies were adopted.Farmers started to usemachinery and an ample amount of chemicals, which marked the beginning of the Green Revolution that improved the productivity and effectiveness of land and farmers. However, the large amount of applications of chemicals and excessive use of machinery cause environmental losses, an increase in pollution, decay of natural resources, and excess uses of electrical energy \[[5](#9789815274349-C2.html#r5)\]. In the 20th century, the agriculture process became more effective and productive due to advanced changes in technology such as robotics arms, autonomous tractors, autonomous harvesters, and drones. Also, due to research-based work in various domains of agriculture, such as soil quality, water quality, pesticide quality, and pest control technology, farming has become commercial (Fig. [**3**](#9789815274349-C2.html#f3)). Smart farming is revolutionizing the agricultural industry by leveraging artificial intelligence, blockchain and the Internet of Things (IoT). With real-time programming embedded in IoT devices, farmers can receive timely and accurate insights into their crops, livestock, and other agricultural factors, enabling them to make smart and productive intelligent decisions that lead to better yields and profitability \[[6](#9789815274349-C2.html#r6)\]. The use of analytics techniques in smart farming allows for the extrapolation of real-time data, providing farmers with comprehensive overviews of their agricultural situations. Such information can be used further to monitor crop health, detect and prevent disease outbreaks, optimize irrigation and fertilization, and manage livestock more effectively. By integrating artificial intelligence concepts into smart farming, farmers can automate repetitive tasks, reduce waste, and optimize resource usage. Artificial Intelligence algorithms can be used to study and analyze historical or past data and make predictions about future crop yields, weather patterns, and other factors that impact agricultural production. In short, big data in smart farming is transforming the agricultural industry by providing farmers with the tools they need to make effective decisions. With real-

time data analytics, artificial intelligence, and IoT devices, farmers can optimize their production processes, reduce costs, and improve their bottom line.

![](Images/9789815274349-C2-F3.jpg)**Fig. (3))**  
Agricultural growth in india \[[7](#9789815274349-C2.html#r7)\]. 

The present agriculture era is smart farming, which optimizes the productivity and effectiveness of land and farmers to a large extent, involving the usage of the latest technologies, such as the Internet of Things, machine learning, Blockchain, artificial intelligence, cloud architecture, wireless remote devices, information security, *etc.* Smart farming reduces environmental effects and also aims to reduce the use of water resources, energy consumption, soil degradation, labor costs, *etc.* Big data and IoT in smart farming give real-time agriculture situations on the basis of which farmers can solve real-time problems and make effective decisions. Real-time programming is developed using artificial concepts, which can be embedded in various IoT devices \[[7](#9789815274349-C2.html#r7)\]. Sustainable agriculture provides an approach that mainly focuses on environmental, social, and economic sustainability. In sustainable agriculture, farming methods utilize natural resources, reduce waste, and promote biodiversity along with productivity and profitability

## SUSTAINABLE AGRICULTURE METHODS

The following are the common sustainable agriculture methods (Fig. [**4**](#9789815274349-C2.html#f4)).

![](Images/9789815274349-C2-F4.jpg)**Fig. (4))**  
Common sustainable agriculture methods.  

1.  **Crop rotation:** In crop rotation methodology, farmers can alternate the crop over a time period, which reduces soil erosion, enhances soil fertility, and controls pests and diseases.
2.  **Conservation cultivation:** Conservation cultivation in sustainable agriculture reduces soil erosion, conserves moisture, and improves soil health.
3.  **Agroforestry:** This includes integrating trees and shrubs into agricultural land to increase benefits such as windbreaks, shade, and wildlife habitat while also improving crop yields.
4.  **Integrated pest management (IPM):** This methodology combines cultural, biological, and chemical methods to manage diseases in crops.
5.  **Organic farming:** Organic farming makes use of natural fertilizers, biological pest control, and crop rotation to grow crops and avoids the use of synthetic chemicals or genetically modified organisms.
6.  **Cover cropping:** This involves planting cover crops, such as legumes or grasses, during fallow periods to prevent soil erosion, fix nitrogen, and improve soil health.
7.  **Precision farming:** The application of technology in precision farming aims to enhance crop productivity while minimizing the use of resources like water, fertilizers, and pesticides.

## FRAMEWORK FOR AGRICULTURE DECISION SUPPORT

The diagram Fig. ([**5**](#9789815274349-C2.html#f5)) shows the main components of agricultural decision support. It has the following main components:

![](Images/9789815274349-C2-F5.jpg)**Fig. (5))**  
Framework for agriculture decision support. 

**Data Collection:** The system collects various types of data related to agriculture, such as weather conditions, soil quality, crop growth, pests, disease outbreaks, and market prices. The data can be collected through various sources such as sensors, drones, satellite imagery, and other relevant tools.

**Data Storage and Management:** The system stores and manages the collected data in a centralized database or data warehouse. The data is processed and analyzed to generate insights that can be used to make informed decisions.

**Analysis and Modeling:** The system uses various analytical tools and models to generate insights from the collected data. These models can include predictive models, simulation models, and optimization models. The models are used to evaluate various scenarios and provide recommendations based on the desired outcomes.

**Decision-Making Support:** The system provides decision-making support by presenting the generated insights and recommendations to the end-users. This information can be presented through dashboards, reports, and other visualizations that are easy to understand and interpret.

**Integration:** The system can integrate with other software and tools that are used in agriculture, such as farm management software, precision agriculture tools, and other relevant systems. This integration enables the ADSS to work seamlessly with other systems and provide a comprehensive view of the agriculture landscape.

**User Interface:** The system provides an easy-to-use and intuitive interface that allows users to interact with the system and access the generated insights and recommendations. The user interface can be accessed through desktop applications, web-based applications, and mobile devices.

**Training and Support:** To ensure effective and efficient usage of the system, training and support are provided to end-users. Smart farming is a revolutionary agricultural technique that leverages the power of information. Smart farming employs various information technologies and communication technologies (ICT) to optimize agricultural labor needs and enhance the quality and quantity of farm produce. This method utilizes contemporary ICT technologies, including the Internet of Things, GPS, sensors, robotics, drones, precision equipment, actuators, and data analytics, to provide customized solutions to farmers' requirements \[[8](#9789815274349-C2.html#r8), [9](#9789815274349-C2.html#r9)\]. In addition to training programs, smart farming also offers customer support services and documentation. By improving the precision and promptness of decision making, smart farming significantly boosts crop productivity. It is not surprising that smart farming has been embraced by several multilateral organizations and developing countries worldwide as a means of increasing agricultural output \[[10](#9789815274349-C2.html#r10)\].

## TECHNOLOGIES USED IN SMART FARMING

Smart farming, commonly known as precision agriculture, involves the utilization of advanced technologies to optimize crop production to enhance farming practices, reduce waste, and increase efficiency in farming operations. Some of the key technologies used in smart farming include:

**1 Internet of Things (IoT):** Electronic devices such as cameras, sensors, and drones are used to collect data on weather, soil moisture, and crop health. This data is analyzed to provide insights and recommendations for crop management.

**2\. Artificial Intelligence (AI):** AI algorithms can be used to analyze historical or past data and also identify patterns, allowing farmers to optimize crop production, reduce waste, and increase efficiency.

**3\. Blockchain:** Blockchain technology is the stream that is concerned about securities and can be used to keep track and verify the origin of food products, increasing transparency and trust in the supply chain.

### Internet of Things (IoT)

The Internet of Things is a system in which physical objects, electronic devices, buildings, and many other items are equipped with sensors, actuators, application and system software, and connectivity to gather and exchange data over the Internet. In an IoT system, devices and objects are connected to the internet and to each other, allowing for the transfer of data and the ability to control and automate devices remotely. This creates an ecosystem of devices and systems that can communicate with each other to provide intelligent insights and automate actions \[[11](#9789815274349-C2.html#r11)\]. Some common IoT devices include smart thermostats, home security systems, smart lock systems, smart home or industrial appliances, wearable fitness trackers, actuators, and industrial sensors. These devices can collect data such as temperature, humidity, location, and movement and send that data to other devices or to a central hub where it can be analyzed and used to make decisions. IoT technology has many potential advantages, such as improved efficiency, cost savings, heightened productivity, and better decision-making. However, it also raises concerns about security, privacy, and the potential for the misuse of personal data. With the ongoing evaluation and widespread adaptation of IoT technology, it will be crucial to address them and guarantee that the benefits of this technology are realized in a safe and responsible manner \[[12](#9789815274349-C2.html#r12)\]. The Internet of Things (IoT) and smart farming are two technologies that are transforming the agriculture industry. IoT involves the interconnection of devices and sensors to collect and share data, while smart farming includes the use of actuators, sensors, embedded systems, drones, and other technologies to optimize crop yields and reduce waste \[[13](#9789815274349-C2.html#r13)\]. Here's a diagram that illustrates the relationship between IoT and smart farming (Fig. [**6**](#9789815274349-C2.html#f6)).

![](Images/9789815274349-C2-F6.jpg)**Fig. (6))**  
IoT and smart farming. 

**Sensors and Devices:** The first step in smart farming is to use sensors, actuators, and devices to collect useful information on various aspects of the farming process, including the condition of weather, soil moisture, and crop growth. These sensors can be connected to the internet using wireless or wired networks.

**Data Collection and Processing:** Once the useful information is collected by the sensors, it is transmitted to a centralized database or cloud platform for analysis. IoT platforms are used to collect, store, and process data in real time.

**Data Analytics and Insights:** By utilizing big data analytics and machine learning algorithms, the information gathered by the sensors can be scrutinized to reveal patterns and valuable insights. This enables farmers to make knowledgeable decisions regarding crop cultivation, irrigation, fertilization, and harvesting \[[14](#9789815274349-C2.html#r14)\].

**Automation and Control:** Smart farming also involves the use of automation technologies, such as drones, robots, and autonomous vehicles, for crop monitoring, harvesting and irrigation. These technologies can be controlled remotely using IoT platforms, which allows farmers to gain productive ways like optimizing crop yields and reducing waste.

**Optimization and Sustainability:** The ultimate goal of smart farming is to optimize crop yields while reducing waste and minimizing the environmental impact of agriculture. By using IoT to collect and analyze data, automate tasks, and optimize crop yields, farmers can achieve these goals more efficiently and sustainably \[[15](#9789815274349-C2.html#r15)\].

In summary, IoT and smart farming are two technologies that are transforming the agriculture industry. By interconnecting devices and sensors to collect and share data and using this data to automate tasks and optimize crop yields, these technologies are helping to ensure that we can feed a growing global population in a sustainable and efficient way \[[16](#9789815274349-C2.html#r16)\].

### Artificial Intelligence (AI)

The ability of machines to perform tasks that usually require human intelligence, like learning, problem solving, and decision making is called artificial intelligence (AI). AI is powered by complex algorithms, statistical models, and neural networks that enable machines to learn from data and improve their performance over time \[[17](#9789815274349-C2.html#r17)\].

**Machine Learning:** Machine learning, an AI subset, employs algorithms that allow machines to learn from data without explicit programming. It can be supervised, where labeled data trains the machine, or unsupervised, where the machine discovers patterns and relationships in unlabeled data.

**Deep Learning:** Deep learning is a type of machine learning that involves the use of neural networks to enable machines to learn and improve their performance over time. Deep learning algorithms are particularly effective at processing unstructured data such as images, videos, and natural language.

**Computer Vision:** Computer vision gives the ability to machines to interpret and understand visual information. Computer vision algorithms are being used in facial recognition, image recognition, and object detection applications.

**Expert Systems:** AI applications known as expert systems are created to replicate the decision-making capabilities of human experts in particular fields. These systems find application in several fields, including medical diagnosis, financial planning, and other areas where human expertise is vital.

Combinedly, artificial intelligence (AI) and smart farming are two technologies that are transforming the agriculture industry \[[18](#9789815274349-C2.html#r18)\]. AI algorithms in machine learning models, such as neural networks, decision trees, and support vector machines, are used for tasks like image recognition, natural language processing, and predictive analytics in agriculture. AI algorithms can be used to analyze historical or past data and also identify patterns and insights, while smart farming involves the use of sensors, drones, and other technologies to optimize crop yields and reduce waste. Here is a diagram Fig. ([**7**](#9789815274349-C2.html#f7)) that illustrates the relationship between AI and smart farming:

![](Images/9789815274349-C2-F7.jpg)**Fig. (7))**  
AI and smart farming diagram. 

**Data Collection:** The first step in smart farming involves collecting useful data from various sources, such as weather sensors, soil sensors, and crop sensors. This data is then transmitted to a centralized database or cloud platform for analysis.

**Data Processing:** Once the data has been collected, it is time to analyze it using AI algorithms. This involves identifying patterns and trends in the data and using this information to optimize crop yields and reduce waste.

**Decision Making:** Based on the insights gained from AI analysis, various decisions about crop planting, irrigation, fertilization, and harvesting can be made. With the help of this approach, crop yields can be optimized in an efficient and sustainable way \[[19](#9789815274349-C2.html#r19)\]

**Automation:** Smart farming also involves the use of automation technologies, such as drones, robots, and autonomous vehicles, to perform tasks such as crop monitoring, irrigation, and harvesting. Round the clock, these technologies can operate independently, eliminating the need for human intervention and optimizing crop yields while reducing waste.

**Optimization:** The ultimate goal of smart farming is to optimize crop yields while reducing waste and minimizing the environmental impact of agriculture. By using AI to analyze data and automate tasks, farmers can achieve these goals more efficiently and sustainably.

In summary, AI and smart farming are two technologies that are transforming the agriculture industry. By collecting and analyzing data, automating tasks, and optimizing crop yields, these technologies are helping to ensure that we can feed a growing global population in a sustainable and efficient way \[[20](#9789815274349-C2.html#r20), [21](#9789815274349-C2.html#r21)\].

### Blockchain

Blockchain technology is used to secure and maintain transparency in transactions. Blockchain provides a transparent and immutable ledger of transactions, allowing farmers to track their products through the supply chain. This transparency is useful for farmers to get paid fairly for their products based on accurate and important data \[[22](#9789815274349-C2.html#r22)\]

**Transaction Data:** The first step in using blockchain is to create a transaction. This can be any type of transaction, such as a financial transaction or the transfer of digital assets.

**Transaction Verification:** Once a transaction has been created, it needs to be verified. This is done through a process called mining, in which network participants solve complex mathematical puzzles to verify the transaction.

**Transaction Block:** Once a transaction has been verified, it is added to a block. A block is a group of verified transactions that are added to the blockchain in chronological order.

**Block Hash:** Each block in the blockchain is assigned a unique hash, which is a digital fingerprint of the block's contents. This hash is used to identify the block and ensure that it has not been tampered with.

**Blockchain Consensus:** In order for a block to be added to the blockchain, it must first be validated by the network. This is done through a consensus algorithm, which ensures that all network participants agree on the state of the blockchain.

**Distributed Ledger:** A blockchain is a distributed ledger that is decentralized and stored across a network of computers instead of a single, centralized location, making it more secure and less susceptible to tampering. This is because any modifications to the blockchain would require a majority consensus from the network's computers.

**Smart Contracts:** Smart contracts, which have their terms coded into them, are self-executing agreements that can be utilized to automate operations and decrease reliance on intermediaries. Blockchain technology can also be used to develop and implement smart contracts. Smart contracts can be executed on the blockchain, allowing for the automation of processes and the reduction of intermediaries. The diagram above illustrates how blockchain works, including transaction data, verification, block creation, block hashing, consensus, distributed ledger, and smart contracts. Blockchain technology can be applied to smart farming to increase transparency, efficiency, and traceability in the agricultural supply chain. Here is a diagram that illustrates how blockchain can be used in smart farming:

**Farm Data Collection:** In order to use blockchain technology in smart farming, data must first be collected from the farm \[[23](#9789815274349-C2.html#r23)\]**.** This can include data on crop yield, soil quality, weather conditions, and more. This data can be collected using sensors, drones, and other IoT devices.

**Data Encryption:** Once the farm data is collected, it needs to be encrypted to ensure its security and privacy. This can be done using encryption algorithms, which encode the data which can be accessed only by authorized receivers.

**Blockchain Transaction:** The encrypted farm data is then added to the blockchain as a transaction. Each transaction is verified by the network of blockchain participants, ensuring its validity and accuracy.

**Smart Contracts:**They are used in smart farming to automate processes such as crop insurance, supply chain management, and more. These contracts are self-

executing and can be triggered by certain events or conditions, reducing the need for intermediaries.

**Traceability and Transparency:** By using blockchain technology in smart farming, the agricultural supply chain becomes more transparent and traceable \[[24](#9789815274349-C2.html#r24)\]. Consumers can easily trace the origin of their food and ensure that it is produced in a sustainable and ethical manner.

**Data Analytics:** The data collected from smart farming can be analyzed using data analytics tools to identify trends, optimize production, and make informed decisions.

In summary, blockchain technology can be used in smart farming to increase transparency, efficiency, and traceability in the agricultural supply chain. The process starts with collecting farm data, which is then encrypted and added to the blockchain as a transaction. The following diagram Fig. ([**8**](#9789815274349-C2.html#f8)) shows the relation between blockchain and smart farming.

![](Images/9789815274349-C2-F8.jpg)**Fig. (8))**  
Blockchain and smart farming diagram. 

## APPLICATIONS IN AGRICULTURE

There are many applications of technology in agriculture that can benefit farmers and increase efficiency and sustainability. Some of the most common applications in agriculture include the following:

**Precision Agriculture:** It uses technologies like GPS, drones, and sensors to collect data on soil, weather, and crop growth, thereby farmers can take decisions about fertilization, irrigation, and pest control. This helps to optimize crop yields and reduce waste.

**Farm Management Software:** Farm management software helps farmers manage their operations more efficiently by providing tools for tracking inventory, scheduling tasks, managing finances, and analyzing data \[[20](#9789815274349-C2.html#r20)\].

**Automated Equipment:** Automated equipment such as robotic milkers, automated irrigation systems, and self-driving tractors can reduce the need for human labor and increase efficiency.

**Biotechnology:** Biotechnology is used to create crops that are resistant to pests, diseases, and environmental stresses, as well as to develop new and improved plant varieties.

**Food Safety and Traceability:** Technologies such as blockchain, DNA testing, and food safety software can be used to ensure the safety and quality of food products and track their origin and journey through the supply chain.

**Agricultural Robotics:** Harvesting, sowing, and planting can be performed by robots, thereby reducing the need for manual labor and increasing efficiency.

**Smart Irrigation Systems:** By using sensors and weather data, smart irrigation systems help farmers in

1\. Optimizing irrigation

2\. Reducing water waste

3\. Increasing crop yields.

**Aquaponics and Hydroponics:** Aquaponics and hydroponics are sustainable farming methods that use water instead of soil to grow crops, reducing water usage and increasing yields. The following diagram Fig. ([**9**](#9789815274349-C2.html#f9)) shows the applications in agriculture.

## ROLE OF ENGINEER IN DEVELOPING SMART FARMING

Engineers play a crucial role in the development and implementation of smart farming technologies. They are responsible for designing, developing, and testing the technologies used in precision agriculture, as well as analyzing and interpreting the data collected by these technologies to provide insights and recommendations to farmers. Some specific roles that engineers play in smart farming include

![](Images/9789815274349-C2-F9.jpg)**Fig. (9))**  
Applications in agriculture. 

**Designing and Developing Smart Farming Technologies:** Engineers work on designing and developing technologies such as sensors, IoT devices, and automated equipment that are used in precision agriculture.

**Testing and Validating Technologies:** Engineers conduct testing and validation of smart farming technologies to ensure that they are functioning properly and delivering accurate data.

**Developing and Implementing Software Solutions:** Engineers develop software solutions such as farm management software, big data analytics platforms, and AI algorithms that are used in precision agriculture.

**Providing Technical Support:** Engineers provide technical support to farmers who are using smart farming technologies, helping them to troubleshoot issues and optimize their use of the technology.

**Analyzing Data and Providing Recommendations:** Engineers analyze data collected by smart farming technologies and use that data to provide insights and recommendations to farmers on how to optimize crop production and reduce waste.

**Ensuring Data Security and Privacy:** Engineers ensure that smart farming technologies are designed and implemented in a way that protects the security and privacy of data collected from farms.

Overall, the role of engineers in smart farming is essential to the development and implementation of technologies that can help farmers increase efficiency, reduce waste, and improve sustainability in the agriculture industry.

## CONCLUSION

Smart farming technologies have the potential to revolutionize the way we produce food, increase efficiency and productivity, reduce waste, and protect the environment. These technologies include a wide range of tools like, drones, robots, sensors, artificial intelligence, and big data, which can help farmers make better decisions and optimize their operations. One of the main advantages of smart farming technologies is the ability to collect and analyze real-time data about crop growth, soil moisture, weather patterns, and other relevant factors. This information can be used to monitor crop health, identify potential problems, and adjust irrigation and fertilizer applications to optimize crop yields. In addition, smart farming technologies can also help reduce the environmental impact of agriculture. For example, precision farming practices can help minimize the use of water and pesticides while improving soil health and biodiversity. However, implementing smart farming technologies can also be a challenge for small-scale farmers who do not have technical expertise to adopt these technologies. In addition, there are concerns about data privacy and security, as well as the potential for these technologies to exacerbate existing inequalities in the agricultural sector. Overall, smart farming technologies hold great promise for the future of agriculture, but careful consideration must be given to the potential benefits and risks, as well as the need for equitable access and implementation.

# Prediction for Increasing Yield Production with IoT and AI Using Soil Properties

  
  
**Aravind H.S.^([1](#9789815274349-C3.html#aff1))****,** **Savitha Ambliihalli Chandrappa^([1](#9789815274349-C3.html#aff1), [\*](#9789815274349-C3.html#cor1))****,** **Neha Venkatesh^([2](#9789815274349-C3.html#aff2))****,** **Kalyan Kumar Basavaiah^([3](#9789815274349-C3.html#aff3))**  
¹ Department of Electronics and Communication, J.S.S. Academy of Technical Education, Bengaluru, India  
² Veterinary Doctor, Government of Karnataka, Shivamogga, India  
³ Departments of Electrical Power Engineering, University of Technology and Applied Science, Muscat, Oman  
  

## Abstract

'Wireless Sensor Networks and 'The Internet of Things' are the two imminent commonalities in agricultural science that allow the development of less exclusive systems to install, control, and maintain low-power standard protocols. The work makes use of low-cost sensors and IoT platforms to help farmers improve agricultural systems with better yield and reduce insufficient usage of water. Precision agriculture helps in terms of quality of yield, efficiency of product, decrease in the environmental harness, and minimal usage of natural assets. The proposed precision model obtains raw properties of the given soil and achieves an overall accuracy of 93.33% in predicting the ideal crop that can be cultivated for the given soil sample using the KNN algorithm and develops a continuous crop monitoring system for the expected crop based on the predefined crop properties.

**Keywords:** Arduino, CSV, Digital farming, Energy-efficient, Food processing, GPS, Green Revolution, IoT, Multi-layer perception SMS, Precision agriculture.

## INTRODUCTION

As technologies evolve, modern agriculture mainly depends on physical sciences and engineering technology. Precision agriculture deals with the automation, computing, and monitoring of the crop \[[1](#9789815274349-C3.html#r1), [2](#9789815274349-C3.html#r2)\]. With the help of promising technology, precision agriculture incessantly replaces the existing farming schemes with modern automatic sustainable agriculture. With this new invention, modern agriculture integrates WSN, GPS, Wi-Fi, IoT, and GIS \[[3](#9789815274349-C3.html#r3)\]. The term “ubiquitous” means “everywhere” and is defined by various attributes. Ubiquitous

computing senses environment changes and automatically adapts and acts based on these changes and preferences. For crop productivity, soil management is one of the components of environmental-human health sustainability. Fertilizers required for protecting the agriculture field are identified and tested. Crop classification can be done through the spatially distributed information that is provided by satellite remote sensing. The first and foremost thing in agricultural planning is to choose a suitable crop that gives maximum yield. Crop production rate is influenced by a number of factors. To gain maximum yield, different records about the various types of crops over seasons are analyzed. With the help of the predicted values and suitable crops, the maximum yield can be obtained. Mobility plays an important role in IoT applications involving management and monitoring systems with real-time scenarios. Many computer algorithms are used in a variety of real-world applications. They are used to assist farmers in providing a general understanding of the growth, performance, and rise in the yield of their crops. The “KNN (K Nearest Neighbor)” algorithm is utilized in the proposed work to create a precision model that predicts the appropriate crop that can be grown in a given kind of soil. One of the essential elements of agriculture is soil. We can quickly determine the nutritional needs of the soil, including the type of soil, amount of water needed, and fertilizer requirements, by examining its properties \[[4](#9789815274349-C3.html#r4)\]. Different types of sensors are available in modern technology to assist in selecting the right soil for specific crops. These recent technical advancements aid farmers in comprehending the characteristics of the soil in which a specific crop thrives and increases agricultural yield.

### Contributions of the Research Article

The objectives of the research article are:

 

*   A comprehensive analysis of the given properties of soil is done, and the ideal crop that can be cultivated in the given soil is predicted.
*   Accessibility of a modular apparatus that is built with cheap components and provides management and automation in the agriculture field.
*   Finally, we develop a management model for precise crops.

## LITERATURE REVIEW

Various papers on new technologies are reviewed for precision agriculture, including machine learning, IoT, sensors, AI, data analytics, etc. The literature survey is categorized into two main sub-sections. (i) WSN/IoT and (ii) Data Analytics for appropriate crop production.

### Wireless Sensor Network (WSN) /Internet of Things (IoT)

WSN has diverse applications in the field of agriculture, which include soil conditions, remote monitoring, watering, and monitoring environmental conditions. A number of authors' contributions are discussed. In order to manage and keep an eye on the agricultural field for rural services, the author put in place a scalable sensor network \[[5](#9789815274349-C3.html#r5)\]. The proposed system is implemented with the help of IoT and MAC components. The network components are analyzed, and improvements are made in all respects. The use of MAC routing protocol increases the throughput in terms of increase in energy efficiency with less delay. Along with this component, Wi-Fi LD (Wi-Fi Long Distance) and fog computing are used \[[6](#9789815274349-C3.html#r6)\]. In Himachal-Pradesh, for agriculture, they used WSN to configure the Decision Support System (DSS), which was used to identify 'Apple Scab' \[[7](#9789815274349-C3.html#r7)\]. To detect this, mill tables were employed. An article highlights how smart, powerful, and inexpensive individual sensor nodes work together to form a collaborative group that aids in crop production \[[8](#9789815274349-C3.html#r8), [9](#9789815274349-C3.html#r9)\]. These sensor nodes store the data, aggregate it for further processing, and analyze it for the improvement of croplands \[[10](#9789815274349-C3.html#r10), [11](#9789815274349-C3.html#r11)\]. WSN exemplifies IoT technology, which is used for the collection of intelligent devices for environmental applications. Furthermore, in the cloud computing approach, processing and analyzing the sensor data for the proper decision minimize natural resources. This system has a collection of devices, storage systems, sensors, and composition of IoT \[[12](#9789815274349-C3.html#r12)^(\-)[15](#9789815274349-C3.html#r15)\]. The entire complex management is part of the Big Data system. An in-depth examination of the smart monitoring system's sample methodology was conducted. \[[16](#9789815274349-C3.html#r16)\].

### Data Analytics

The prior technique involves human error due to manual estimation of quantity and crop loss \[[17](#9789815274349-C3.html#r17)\]. This was due to the ignorance of the inspectors. The application of machine learning in the agricultural sector has solved this. In the agricultural field, the solution comes from using ML and data analytics for large amounts of data. It provides the optimal answer for obscure factors that impact horticulture, such as temperature, humidity, and soil moisture. For the purpose of analyzing pests and predicting yield diseases, artificial neural networks (ANNs), support vector machines (SVMs), logistic regression (LR), and fuzzy technology are employed. A classification approach for apple ailments was proposed by Singh S. *et al.* using machine learning \[[18](#9789815274349-C3.html#r18)\]. Two diseases are categorized using images of apple leaves: “Apple scab” and “Marsonina”. SVM, K Nearest Neighbor, and Naïve Bayes are used to categorize illnesses. The suggested arrangement is simulated using Matlab. Its accuracy in classifying illnesses is 99.4%. Shinde S.S. et al., in a study, discussed the current systems that are unreliable and expensive \[[19](#9789815274349-C3.html#r19)\]. Trilles S. *et al.* advised using machine learning to forecast the affordable crop for specified input parameters \[[20](#9789815274349-C3.html#r20)\]. The suggested effort will provide farmers with practical ideas for more lucrative farming. The crop is accurately predicted by the suggested supervised machine learning method using a naïve Bayes Gaussian classifier. For the specified input parameters, the seed is thus projected as an output. The underprivileged who lack the expertise of farmers in crop prediction for the development of a sustainable future may benefit immensely from this work. The Industrial Revolution has resulted in a steady decline in agriculture's economic contribution to India's GDP, notwithstanding the country's broad-based economic expansion. Rainfall and temperature have the greatest effects on crop yield forecast, among other elements. Thus, improved yield can be obtained by applying contemporary machine learning techniques. The study takes into account external influences and uses machine learning algorithms to assist farmers in selecting the crop that would provide the highest yield \[[21](#9789815274349-C3.html#r21)\]. The best classifier, according to the results, is a random forest when all the parameters are combined. When compared to all other machine learning methods, it has the lowest mean absolute error and provides greater accuracy than any other method. Because of its high oil content (over 20%) and high protein content (above 40%), soybean is an important food item \[[22](#9789815274349-C3.html#r22)\]. The illness is the cause of the low yield issue facing the nation's soy industry. Using machine learning algorithms, the categorization of soybean diseases is carried out while taking meteorological conditions and physical crop characteristics into account. In terms of accuracy, the neural network model performs better than other machine learning methods that only use one classifier. However, it provides greater accuracy when compared to the “ensemble classifier”. Various authors have determined that a single classifier is unable to produce a result that is accurate enough. As a result, to categorize the disorders, we employed a decision tree and a “Bagging ensemble classifier”. It is crucial to identify the insects' existence in a given area as soon as possible because this allows for the tracking of their spread. Using mobile phones, we may provide decision-makers with useful feedback by introducing machine learning approaches to help them choose the best management measures for insects \[[23](#9789815274349-C3.html#r23)\]. Faster CNN with Mobile NetV3 backbone is a suitable option because of its strong baseline for insect detection, good performance, and quick inference speed on both CPU and GPU. Our model produced the fewest false positives for the insect class, which is important for this kind of application.

## FRAMEWORK OF THE PROPOSED SYSTEM

Fig. ([**1**](#9789815274349-C3.html#f1)) depicts the suggested architecture for precision agriculture. There are several phases that explain the suggested system. Sensors that measure pH, moisture, temperature, and humidity are necessary in modern agriculture in order to gather the raw data needed for crop growth. Sensor electrodes function by identifying particular soil ions. The pH sensor is useful for determining a solution's “acidity” or “alkalinity” within a range of 0–14. Estimating the total amount of stored water in the soil can be aided by measuring the moisture sensor and soil water content. Soil water is not directly measured by soil moisture sensors. Rather, they track variations in a few other soil characteristics that are correlated with water content in a predictable way. Devices that can transform temperature and humidity into electrical impulses so that “temperature” and “humidity” can be easily measured are known as temperature and humidity sensors or pH temp sensors.

![](Images/9789815274349-C3-F1.jpg)**Fig. (1))**  
Proposed architecture for WSN-based precision agriculture via IoT. 

### Components of Sub-modules

#### Arduino UNO

The Arduino UNO is an inexpensive, customizable, and versatile electronic board that manages the motor's output, relays, and LED.

#### Internet of Things (IoT)

IoT is the collective network that makes it easier for devices to communicate with each other and with the cloud.

#### Machine Learning (ML)

Without explicit programming, software-based systems can get increasingly accurate at predicting outcomes thanks to machine learning (ML). One approach for grouping data is KNN. It is adept at grouping unlabeled data according to similarities (k) into a fixed number of clusters.

#### Servo Motor

Precise angular location control is provided by this actuator.

#### LCD Display

The 16x2 LCD module can show 32 characters (16 characters per line) and has a total of 16 pins. It is employed for output display.

#### Sensors

The sensors gather unprocessed data from the soil, which is then transmitted to Arduino for additional processing. The 16x2 display shows the raw data from the sensors via an Arduino code. For later study, the exhibited data is saved in CSV format on a cloud platform (Google Drive).

Fig. ([**2**](#9789815274349-C3.html#f2)) illustrates the methodology's graphical portrayal. An Arduino UNO is connected to a soil sensor in order to collect data. Cloud storage is used to store data. The KNN Machine Learning algorithm is used to perform the prediction in the background. Farmers receive an SMS with the processed predicted crop details at the end.

![](Images/9789815274349-C3-F2.jpg)**Fig. (2))**  
Pictorial representation of methodology. 

### Flowchart of the Proposed System

The system proposed is implemented in two stages. First, using the sensors, the pH, temperature, humidity, and moisture levels are calculated for the given soil sample. Through IoT, these data values are sent in CSV format to the cloud (Google Drive). We predict the kind of crop that can be grown using the sensed data by using the KNN algorithm. Via SMS, farmers receive the forecasted crop information in addition to the soil parameters. Only the moisture content of the soil is taken into account in the second stage. The servo motor switches on, and the LED turns red, signaling low moisture if the moisture reading is less than the minimum moisture value. The servo motor turns off, and the LED turns green if the moisture value is higher than the minimum moisture value. The flowchart for the suggested precision agricultural system is displayed in Fig. ([**3**](#9789815274349-C3.html#f3)).

![](Images/9789815274349-C3-F3.jpg)**Fig. (3))**  
Flowchart of the proposed system. 

## IMPLEMENTATION OF THE PROPOSED SYSTEM

The proposed precision agriculture system was implemented successfully. The initial step is concerned with the data collection. In this phase, the perception of IOT is used. Sensors such as pH sensors, DHT11sensor, and soil moisture sensors are used to get the water, weather, and soil data, respectively. The pH sensor is utilized to know the alkaline nature of water based on pH value. DHT11sensor is used to get the atmosphere's humidity and temperature values, and the contents of oil water are measured by a soil moisture sensor. The suggested precision agriculture approach was effectively put into practice. The data collecting is the focus of the first step. During this stage, the IOT perception is applied. The water, weather, and soil data are obtained, respectively, by sensors such as pH sensors, DHT11 sensors, and soil moisture sensors. Using the pH value as a guide, the pH sensor determines the alkaline nature of water. A soil moisture sensor measures the contents of oil water, and a DHT11 sensor measures the temperature and humidity of the atmosphere.

![](Images/9789815274349-C3-F4.jpg)**Fig. (4))**  
Schematic diagram used in the proposed system. 

## RESULTS AND DISCUSSION

In the lab, soil analysis was done, and the pH values of the corresponding soils were recorded with a pH meter, as seen in Fig. ([**5**](#9789815274349-C3.html#f5)). The calibration of the pH sensor was done, which is shown in Figs. ([**6**](#9789815274349-C3.html#f6), [**7**](#9789815274349-C3.html#f7) and [**8**](#9789815274349-C3.html#f8)). When the electrode was at the first stage of the solution droplets, it waswashed quickly with a rinse solution. Following this, the electrode wasshaken in the buffer or sample stabilize the reading.

The pH readings of the related soils were obtained from the pH meter during a soil study that was conducted in the lab. Each type of soil has a unique pH. Using this pH value, we may predict the crops that can be grown in that particular soil. Before we can use the pH sensor to examine the soil, we need to calibrate it. The main objective of the planned endeavor is to investigate the properties of several soil types using soil analysis. Here, three distinct soil types are examined, clay, sandy, and loamy soil. Determining whether the soil is basic or acidic is made easier with the help of the pH value that this analysis provides. Fig. ([**9**](#9789815274349-C3.html#f9)) illustrates the division of the Python script utilized in the suggested task into 4 programs. Using sensors, the first Python program gathers raw soil data from the provided soil sample and transmits it to the cloud. The second software predicts the appropriate crop that can be grown in the provided soil sample using the KNN classification method. During the monitoring phase, the minimum moisture value for the crop is set as the default by the third Python program. It finds minimal moisture for the crop. It keeps an eye on the anticipated crop in relation to the minimal amount of moisture needed for the crop to grow well. The fourth Python application serves as a “reset” command, allowing users/farmers to begin the entire process anew for a different soil sample by erasing all of the data gathered. The user can end a Python application by using the “exit” command.

Twilio APL is used to provide farmers SMS instructions on how to cultivate an appropriate crop based on the soil that is provided. With the use of the aforementioned knowledge, farmers may better understand the water needs of their crops and use less water. Before being sent to an SMS, a copy of the minimum and maximum values for the anticipated crop is kept on the drive. The moisture sensor measures the current moisture content. The servo motor automatically turns on if the measured value is less than the minimum moisture value. A web-based tool called Twilio API facilitates digital interactions via voice and text messaging to increase productivity and results. Building deep connections with customers can begin with an app's integration with any CRM or customer database. Twilio helps to avoid many of the typical complications in the field of telephony by offering a straightforward access point. With Twilio, developers can instantly achieve global connectivity by utilizing straightforward markup and popular internet protocols. The same messaging platforms used for SMS and MMS, Twilio Conversations and Twilio Programmable Messaging, easily integrate with current software and systems to create common WhatsApp use cases. Through Twilio, qualified companies of any size can get access to the most widely used messaging software globally. We made use of Twilio's Free version for the suggested project. In Fig. ([**20**](#9789815274349-C3.html#f20)), the steps involved in creating a Twilio account are displayed. A distinct SID must be assigned to the account, and the password must be kept secret. We provide the registered cell phone numbers of the users for whom we must send the anticipated data under this account. ## CONCLUSION

In response to the increasing demand for food production and the gradual depletion of natural resources, the agriculture industry turned to artificial intelligence (AI) and machine learning (ML) technologies through the use of ingenious and creative Internet gadget applications to maximize production and reduce losses. The work is carried out in two stages. Using sensors (pH, temperature, humidity, and moisture sensors), the initial step entails gathering raw data on the attributes of the soil and storing it on a cloud platform via the Internet of Things technology. A forecast of the kind of crops that can be cultivated based on received raw sensor data along with its minimal and maximum details (pH, Temperature, Humidity, and Moisture) is made with the help of the most straightforward and popular machine learning technique, the “KNN classification algorithm”. The registered farmers receive an SMS with these crop prediction details. In the second stage, ongoing observation is done. The necessary water supply to maintain the least amount of moisture in the soil can be provided by a servo motor, which can also be connected to a water outlet. This precision model predicts the best crop to grow for the provided soil sample with a 93.33% accuracy rate. This can result in improved crop productivity and management by assisting farmers in understanding the minimum requirements (pH, Temperature, Humidity, and Moisture) of their crops. In the future, our system can concentrate on developing a disease management model based on the crops that are planted in that soil. For future considerations, additional factors like temperature, humidity, and pH of the soil may be taken into account. The technology under consideration employs a servo motor. To maintain the soil's moisture content, it can be linked to a water sprinkler. To manage illnesses, pests, and insect infestations, a pesticide is utilized in lieu of a water sprinkler.

# Pesticide Prediction and Disease Identification with AIoT

  
  
**Ajay Kumar Dharmireddy^([1](#9789815274349-C4.html#aff1), [\*](#9789815274349-C4.html#cor1))****,** **Kambham Jacob Silva Lorraine^([1](#9789815274349-C4.html#aff1))****,** **Ravi Kumar Maddumala^([1](#9789815274349-C4.html#aff1))****,** **Kotha Lavanya^([1](#9789815274349-C4.html#aff1))**  
¹ Department of Electronics and Communication Engineering, Sir C.R.R. College of Engineering, Eluru, Andhra Pradesh, India  
  

## Abstract

Agriculture is vital to human survival and has a significant impact on the economy of any nation. Crop protection costs millions of dollars per year. Insects and other pests pose a serious threat to the health of a harvest. Excessive use of chemical fertilizers and pesticides negatively affects the crop and soil quality. Therefore, one way to safeguard the harvest and mitigate potential losses is through early identification of the pests. Examining the crop at the right moment is the best technique to determine its overall health. While manual inspection is the standard way of conducting field inspection, it becomes challenging for large fields. In addition, manual inspection would be exceedingly expensive and tedious. To address this, an automated system is needed to detect pests, identify them, and recommend appropriate fertilizers using an IoT system. Therefore, automated pest detection has become a major focus for researchers globally, as it offers a more efficient and cost-effective alternative to manual inspection. In this work, a smart agriculture system has been proposed that monitors crops, identifies pests, and allows remote control. The dataset comprises over 4000 images of corn leaves, categorized into rust, blight, grey spots, and healthy leaves. By employing Convolutional Neural Networks (CNN), the system has achieved a remarkable 99% accuracy in pest detection.

**Keywords:** Agriculture, Automation, Convolutional Neural Network (CNN), Crop protection, IOT system, Irrigation, Image processing, Pest infestation, Pest identification.  
  

* * *

## INTRODUCTION

India is known as the “land of agriculture”, with the agricultural sector serving as the backbone of the country. A significant 75% of the nation's population is employed in the agricultural sector, underscoring the critical role of producing

high-quality and high-quantity food crops. Various modern techniques are introduced to assist the farmers in achieving this goal. However, farmers continue to face multiple challenges and significant economic losses in creating a better food crop. These challenges include issues with acquiring high-quality fertilizers and seeds, harvest storage, and pest infestation.

Despite governmental solutions for various agricultural issues, pest infestations remain a concern that necessitates an appropriate response to the pest problem. Pests inflict substantial damage to various crops, resulting in significant reductions in crop quality and quantity and, consequently, economic losses. In addressing this issue, farmers have resorted to the use of insecticides. However, a more comprehensive understanding of pests and pesticides is of vital importance before the application of such solutions. A farmer who sprays pesticides without sufficient expertise may encounter many issues. Manual application of pesticides without adequate expertise may lead to skin ailments, respiratory issues, skin cancer, and other health concerns. Moreover, manual approaches not only lead to ineffective results but also pose environmental risks. In addition, as a precaution, farmers resort to spraying pesticides in large quantities, causing harm to crops and the environment.

Various pest identification and monitoring methods have recently been used, including image processing and complex algorithms, for pest detection and classification. The best approach is automatic detection, which uses image processing methods to identify and categorize the pests based on the various attributes of the images. In this study, images of agricultural field leaves were obtained, and then different processing methods were performed. The 'threshold approach' was used to differentiate the normal and diseases portion of the leaves. This approach is straightforward and effective for detecting pests from photos. Different image properties are extracted and used as input for support vector machines (SVM) to classify images with and without pests. This research emphasized white flies that are incredibly minute and complicated to inspect with the human eye yet may cause widespread crop damage. The suggested method detects pests on plants and then calculates how many white flies are on each leaf. This study describes a technique for automatically identifying white flies in leaves. ResNet-18 is a CNN model used for disease detection, known for its skip connection mechanism and 18-layer efficiency and accuracy. The suggested approach has proven to be highly useful for implementing early preventative steps and saving the environment from the adverse effects of widespread pesticide use.

## LITERATURE SURVEY

### Different Techniques for Pest Detection

Different pest detection techniques were explored in a recent study. Three distinct approaches were demonstrated: one using global features from insect images, another using local features, and a third combining both. A hierarchical feature combination was used for classification, with global characteristics at the top level and local features at the bottom. An insect recognition model for green leafhoppers was suggested by H. Nagar and R. S. Sharma using digital photos, employing the colors and forms of insects as identifying characteristics \[[1](#9789815274349-C4.html#r1)\]. P. Rajan proposed a methodology for pest identification that uses color features by dissecting the picture into its component colors (RGB) \[[2](#9789815274349-C4.html#r2)\]. The pixel-to-pixel intensity difference was also calculated. Pixels with values over a certain threshold in any of the three color channels (R, G, or B) are unwanted intrusions. Later, SVM was utilized to classify. Javed *et al.* devised a model for insect detection \[[3](#9789815274349-C4.html#r3)\]. This model's picture segmentation facilitated insect image detection, which was done by refining the K-means segmentation technique. The process of feature extraction makes use of the discrete cosine transform. Using ANN, they were able to improve classification accuracy to 97%.

### Image Processing in the Field of Agricultural Research

Samantha and Ghosh introduced an automated system for identifying insect pests in tea using artificial neural networks \[[4](#9789815274349-C4.html#r4)\]. This research examined the insect pest data from tea estates in the North Bengal Districts of India. They utilized correlation-based feature selection (CFS) and incremental back propagation network (IBPLN). Aphids can cause significant damage in wheat fields, leading to substantial yield losses. Tao Liu proposed a method based on digital image analysis for identifying and monitoring aphid populations. This method offers a user-friendly approach to accurately monitor aphid populations in wheat fields and can contribute to better pest management in wheat farming \[[5](#9789815274349-C4.html#r5)\]. Other researchers have proposed a method to identify infested plant sections, such as roots and leaves. Using data from several cameras, they interpreted scenes and analyzed videos to help make intelligent decisions on the spot. The proposed approach has been employed for apriority detection of aphids and whiteflies. Al-Safer developed a neural network-based identification system for pecan weevils. The method is reliable and can be used to design an automated, wireless sensor network for detecting pecan weevils in the field \[[6](#9789815274349-C4.html#r6)\]. Zhu and Zhang proposed a novel method for identifying insects \[[7](#9789815274349-C4.html#r7)\]. They combined the dual-tree and region-matching complex wavelet transforms in their image-matching work. Images of lepidopteron insects were processed using the mean shift method and the k-means algorithm for feature extraction.G. Shrestha *et al.* categorized 12 plant disorders and identified diseased plants with a rate of 88.80% effectiveness \[[8](#9789815274349-C4.html#r8)\]. Experiments were conducted using a collection of 3000 high-resolution RGB pictures. In this network, the convolutional and pooling layers each include three blocks. As a consequence, the network eventually becomes prohibitively costly. Furthermore, the model's F1 score is 0.12, which is extremely low due to many incorrect pessimistic predictions.

Amin *et al.* proposed a CNN model for maize leaf detection using digital images \[[9](#9789815274349-C4.html#r9)\]. They collected a plant village dataset that consisted of 217,000 images of 38 different kinds of healthy and unhealthy leaves. The researchers utilized data augmentation techniques to enhance the features of input images. In order to achieve higher accuracy, transfer learning techniques were employed. The authors evaluated the model's performance using various parameters such as the Receiver Operating Characteristic (ROC) curve and Area Under the Curve (AUC). The proposed model was able to achieve a classification accuracy of 98.56% using outperforming efficient models like ResNet152, EfficientNetB0, Inceptionv3, and Densenet121.

Zhang *et al.* introduced the use of deep learning-based improved GoogLeNet and Cifar10 models to enhance the accuracy of maize leaf disease recognition while reducing network parameters \[[10](#9789815274349-C4.html#r10)\]. By adjusting parameters, changing pooling combinations, adding dropout operations and rectified linear unit functions, and reducing the number of classifiers, two improved models are trained and tested on nine kinds of maize leaf images. The GoogLeNet model achieves a top-1 average identification accuracy of 98.9% in recognition of eight kinds of maize leaf diseases, while the Cifar10 model achieves an average accuracy of 98.8%. These improved methods have the potential to enhance the accuracy of maize leaf disease identification and reduce convergence iterations.

Charisma and Adhinata proposed the use of deep learning for classifying potato leaf disease \[[11](#9789815274349-C4.html#r11)\]. This study utilizes the DenseNet201 architecture for deep learning. The study involves two scenarios: comparing the number of dropouts and evaluating three different optimizers. The best model is achieved using a dropout of 0.1 and the Adam optimizer, resulting in a training accuracy of 99.5%, validation accuracy of 95.2%, and a 96% accuracy for the confusion matrix. A testing dataset containing 40 images is utilized to evaluate the model's performance, yielding a new accuracy of 92.5% for classifying potato leaf disease.

Crop disease is a major threat to food safety worldwide. Detecting and identifying plant diseases automatically is essential for food security and managing crop yield. In the work done by Kumar et al., a dataset containing 15200 images of crop leaves was used to train a Residual Network (ResNet34) for disease classification \[[12](#9789815274349-C4.html#r12)\]. The resulting ResNet34 model achieved an impressive 99.40% accuracy on a test set, demonstrating the effectiveness of this approach. This demonstrates the potential of using automated networks for large-scale crop disease detection.

Song Kai proposed a cyber color space model for maize leaf disease detection using image processing techniques. The author uses a BP network and spatial gray level matrix to identify and classify diseases in digital images based on texture characteristics. The model's performance is evaluated by comparing sample mean values and neural network output error. This model achieved an accuracy of 98% \[[13](#9789815274349-C4.html#r13)\].

Khirade and Patel employed digital image processing methods and BPNN (back propagation neural networks) to tackle plant disease identification challenges \[[14](#9789815274349-C4.html#r14)\]. The authors devised several ways to recognize plant disease using photos of leaves. They employed Otsu's threshold, followed by border detection and spot detection methods, to segment the contaminated region of the leaf. They then extracted features, including color, texture, morphology, and edges, to categorize plant illnesses. The BPNN algorithm is used to classify and diagnose plant diseases.

Sun *et al.* proposed a feature fusion detection method based on the CNN model to diagnose corn leaf diseases \[[15](#9789815274349-C4.html#r15)\]. The method consists of three key steps: dataset preprocessing, fine-tuning network, and detection module. The model has been optimized with a generalized intersection over union (GIoU) loss function, and after 60000 iterations, the highest mean average precision (mAP) reaches 91.83%. Experimental results show that the improved model outperforms existing methods in terms of precision and frames per second (FPS).

D. A. Padilla *et al.* proposed a model that was implemented by using a convolution neural network model *via* Open Mp for maize leaf disease detection \[[16](#9789815274349-C4.html#r16)\]. By employing CNN and Open MP, the study demonstrates advantages, especially in terms of execution time. The system algorithm was tested using images of diseased corn leaves, verified by an agriculturist and using Raspberry Pi. The study achieved an accuracy of 93%, 89%, and 89% in detecting leaf blight, leaf rust, and leaf spot, respectively. The use of Convolutional Neural Networks via Open MP resulted in a high percentage of successful disease classification.

Liu *et al.* proposed a CNN model designed to identify the four common types of apple leaf diseases \[[17](#9789815274349-C4.html#r17)\]. They used a dataset consisting of 13,689 images. Their experimental results demonstrated that the model achieved an overall accuracy of 97.62% in comparison to other standard models such as Alex Net, GoogLeNet, ResNet-20, and VGG-16. The parameters for the proposed model were decreased by 51,206,928 compared to the Alex Net model, while the accuracy of the resulting pathological images was increased by 10.83%.

Fuentes *et al.* introduced a deep-learning-based approach to detect diseases and pests in tomato plants using images captured in place by camera devices with various resolutions \[[18](#9789815274349-C4.html#r18)\]. To find the more suitable deep-learning architecture for the task, they considered three main families of detectors, all of which are classified as “deep learning meta-architectures”.

 

*   The Faster Region-based CNN (Faster R-CNN)
*   The Area CNN (R-FCN)
*   The Single Action MultiBox Detector (SSMD)

To combine these meta-architectures (ResNet), “deep feature extractors” like VGG net and Residual Network were used. A huge tomato diseases and pest dataset is used for training and testing the systems, which contains challenging images of diseases and pests, including several inter- and extra-class variations, such as infection status and location in the plant.

Wallelign *et al.* focused on utilizing CNN for classifying plant diseases using leaf images captured in natural environments \[[19](#9789815274349-C4.html#r19)\]. The model is based on the LeNet architecture and is designed specifically for classifying soybean plant diseases. The study utilized 12,673 leaf images, including healthy leaves, obtained from the PlantVillage database. The images were taken in uncontrolled environments. The implemented model achieved an impressive 99.32% classification accuracy, demonstrating the CNN's capacity to extract crucial features and accurately classify plant diseases from images taken in natural settings.

Sladojevic *et al.* presented an approach to developing a model for recognizing plant diseases based on leaf image classification using deep convolutional networks \[[20](#9789815274349-C4.html#r20)\]. The developed model can recognize 13 different types of plant diseases in addition to identifying healthy leaves and distinguishing plant leaves from their surroundings. The in-depth CNN training was conducted using Coffee, a deep learning framework created by the Berkley Vision and Learning Centre. The experimental results showed an average accuracy of 96.3%.

Materne and Inoue proposed a smart pest detection system using IoT for crop protection \[[21](#9789815274349-C4.html#r21)\]. The system integrates sensors for monitoring environmental conditions and pest activity. It utilizes machine learning algorithms for pest detection and generates alerts for farmers. Additionally, the system suggests appropriate pest control measures based on the detected pest species.

Hansa Chowdary developed a pest detection system using IoT and Arduino \[[22](#9789815274349-C4.html#r22)\]. The system uses sensors and machine learning algorithms to monitor environmental parameters and detect pests in crops. Data collected by the sensors is sent to a cloud-based server for analysis. When pests are detected, the system alerts the farmer. This system can reduce the use of harmful chemicals in agriculture and provide real-time pest activity information to farmers.

Despite efforts to enhance crop production, harsh environmental conditions and frequent pest infestations result in agricultural losses. To address this, advanced technologies such as sensors and the Internet of Things (IoT) that can boost agricultural production and reduce economic losses were proposed by Rajak *et al.* \[[23](#9789815274349-C4.html#r23)\]. The system employs various sensors such as light, temperature, humidity, and soil moisture sensors. The collected data is processed using machine learning algorithms to detect and classify pests. Real-time alerts are sent to farmers via mobile applications.
