# Framework Based on IoT, AI, and Blockchain for Smart Access to Government Agricultural Schemes

  
  
**D. Vinodha^([1](#9789815274349-C8.html#aff1), [\*](#9789815274349-C8.html#cor1))****,** **M.J. Buvana^([2](#9789815274349-C8.html#aff2))****,** **S. Rajalakshmi^([2](#9789815274349-C8.html#aff2))****,** **J. Jenefa^([1](#9789815274349-C8.html#aff1))****,** **E.A. Mary Anita^([1](#9789815274349-C8.html#aff1))****,** **Maria Lapina^([3](#9789815274349-C8.html#aff3))**  
¹ School of Engineering and Technology, CHRIST (Deemed to be University), Kengeri, Bangalore, India  
² Department of Computer Science and Engineering, Sri Venkateswara College of Engineering, Sriperumbudur, India  
² Institute of Digital Development, North-Caucasus Federal University, Stavropol, Russia  
  

## Abstract

Agriculture plays an important part in most countries, such as India. A survey says that 54.6% of the total labor force of India is engaged in agriculture and its connected activities. The government is announcing many schemes to facilitate agriculture and support farmers. But most of the farmers are from poor families and are not able to reach the government schemes when they are really in need. Also, it is required to observe and measure the inter and intra-field variability in crops to enjoy the complete benefits of government schemes. This can be done with the advancements in the field of the Internet of Things. Information related to the impact of natural calamities on the agricultural field, malfunctions in the machinery used for cropping, yielding level, and health status of crops can be measured using the technology of IoT (Internet of Things) and analyzed using AI (Artificial Intelligence). Blockchain plays a critical role in replacing traditional means of data storage and exchanging agricultural data with a more trustworthy, immutable, transparent, and decentralized approach. By keeping all the transactions related to government schemes in blockchain, the possible crimes in the form of false data by the intermediate dealers acting between the farmers and the government can be addressed. This, in turn, allows useful government schemes to reach the farmer in time. We propose to develop a theoretical model using IoT, AI, and blockchain, which can assist the farmers in benefitting from the appropriate schemes announced by the government in time and achieving precise agriculture.

**Keywords:** Artificial intelligence, Blockchain, Drone monitoring system, Internet of Things, Learning model, Multispectral imaging sensors, Precision agriculture, Unmanned aerial vehicles.  
  

* * *

^(\*) **Corresponding author D. Vinodha:** School of Engineering and Technology, CHRIST (Deemed to be University), Kengeri, Bangalore, India; E-mail: [vinodha.rajkumar@gmail.com](mailto:vinodha.rajkumar@gmail.com)  
  

## INTRODUCTION

Recently, concerns over food production have increased drastically. It is due to the surge in the world population and the decrease in agricultural resources. Many governments and private organizations are trying to find ways to improve agricultural efficiency. This results in increased research and spending on farming. Nowadays, drones and robots are used for agricultural automation in all stages, from soil preparation to harvesting, to increase productivity and quality. Researchers focus on introducing new approaches to increase yield with fewer agricultural resources. Traditional agriculture uses basic methods of cultivation with primitive knowledge and standard tools. More than half of the world's population uses this method for food production. Even though the cost of production is less in conventional agriculture, it has many cons like low productivity, nutrition depletion, compromise in quality, increase in pollution, *etc.* Hence, there is a need for some modern techniques in agriculture to improve crop productivity without compromising the quality of the crops.

Precision agriculture uses more accurate ways of cultivation to increase productivity with high quality. It gathers, analyzes, and manages information based on the soil, crops, and other parameters to yield high productivity \[[1](#9789815274349-C8.html#r1)\]. It is also known as satellite agriculture. It uses sensors to acquire information about the soil. Real-time images of the crops, farm fields, weather conditions, *etc.*, are acquired using satellites, drones, and robots \[[2](#9789815274349-C8.html#r2), [3](#9789815274349-C8.html#r3)\]. Captured images are analyzed further with the sensor data to obtain the necessary information for better cultivation of crops. Agriculture involves many stages, like crop selection, land preparation, seed selection, seed sowing, irrigation, crop growth, fertilizing, and harvesting. Each stage can be automated using drones and robots, which helps in increasing productivity. It also reduces the dependency on weather conditions by creating the best environmental conditions suitable for different crop production. Even though precision farming has many advantages, it is expensive and requires huge capital. It is the main reason that most farmers continue with traditional farming methods. Since over 80% of the farmers are poor, implementing such expensive infrastructure in their small portion of land will be unthinkable for them. In rural areas, there is a difference in the land portions owned by the farmers. Because of the land differences, adapting modern infrastructure is tedious. It also requires prior knowledge to operate the tools, which the farmers must learn. Since they are comfortable with the conventional method, there is a lack of interest in adapting to modern technologies. If they are adopted, understanding and implementing them in the farmlands consume more time and capital. To overcome the drawbacks of precision agriculture mentioned above, the government proposed many schemes to benefit the farmers. To better understand the proposed government schemes, a friendly handbook explaining them and their benefits has also been issued to the farmers by the Department of Agriculture, Cooperation and Farmers Welfare (DAC&FW) and the Ministry of Agriculture and Farmers Welfare. It includes the details of the assistance provided to the farmers to increase productivity. A few details are listed below.

• Assistance to improve soil conditions by distributing micronutrients and soil ameliorants and supplying gypsum/pyrite/lime/dolomite, lime/liming materials, and bio-fertilizers, which are proposed with affordable prices for the benefit of the farmers.

• Water management methods under the Mission for Sustainable Agriculture (MSA) include drip irrigation, sprinkler irrigation, water harvesting systems for individuals, construction of tube wells/bore wells (shallow/medium), *etc.*

• E-marketing of agriculture commodities.

• Assistance with organic farming with bio-chemicals, bio-pesticides, and bio-fertilizers.

• Assistance in the cultivation of horticulture crops.

Though the government has proposed many schemes to help the farmers achieve high productivity, they have not been well received by the farmers. On the other hand, because of the intermediate dealers, the farmers are not getting their actual share for their contribution, which in turn reduces the farmers' trust in government schemes, which may also lead to conflicts, protests, and other severe consequences. Thus, a secure system is needed to exchange the information directly with the farmers rather than through the intermediate dealer. With the system, the farmers can acquire benefits and productivity can be increased. Hence, this paper proposes a model with recent technologies to assist the farmers in getting benefits from the schemes. The paper is organized as follows: Section 2 explicates the existing precision agriculture methods with their merits and demerits. Section 3 describes the technologies used in various stages of farming to increase productivity. A framework of smart access model for accessing government schemes is proposed in Section 4, and Section 5 concludes this paper.

## EXISTING SYSTEMS

With rising population, the problem pertaining to food production has attracted focus. Population with diverse needs is expected to increase to between 9.4 and 10.1 billion by 2050, increasing the need for designated areas for food production \[[4](#9789815274349-C8.html#r4)\]. Human-induced environmental changes may result in circumstances where the growth of new crops is impossible. In the same way, increasing urbanization raises prices, decreases productivity, and reduces labor in regions that are normally involved in food production \[[5](#9789815274349-C8.html#r5)\]. Smart agriculture is a novel concept of agriculture management that applies technical mechanisms at several stages of farming production to address the difficulties associated with the demands for food production and the decline in the workforce \[[6](#9789815274349-C8.html#r6), [7](#9789815274349-C8.html#r7)\]. It makes use of sensing nodes, communication networks, management information systems, and data analysis tools \[[8](#9789815274349-C8.html#r8)\].

The term “Internet of Things” (IoT) is frequently used to define this network of connected gadgets \[[9](#9789815274349-C8.html#r9)\]. By enabling the relevant activities to be taken at the proper time, quantity, and location, the data generated by smart farming aids in increasing output and reducing waste \[[10](#9789815274349-C8.html#r10)\]. Additionally, recent technology advancements in IoT-related fields make it simpler to implement and use smart agriculture with IoT \[[11](#9789815274349-C8.html#r11)\]. These technical advancements comprise network connectivity, hardware size reduction, power usage optimization, and device cost reduction. Additionally, through developing encouragement programs and strategies to support research and training financially, the largest agricultural producers in the world are pushing the use of IoT in smart farming \[[12](#9789815274349-C8.html#r12)\].

IoT solutions for smart agriculture are continually gaining new contributions and improving and are extensively reviewed in several studies \[[13](#9789815274349-C8.html#r13), [14](#9789815274349-C8.html#r14)\]. The authors provide a number of use cases for the cited IoT applications for smart farming. The authors of a study \[[15](#9789815274349-C8.html#r15)\] recognized data visualization techniques and edge computing and communication technologies within these fields. The IoT solutions for smart farming and smart agriculture and the role of sensing devices in the various IOT-based applications with three-layer architecture are analyzed in research studies \[[16](#9789815274349-C8.html#r16), [17](#9789815274349-C8.html#r17)\].

IoT-based real-time agriculture monitoring systems for upgrading irrigation systems and measuring soil moisture in a field have been suggested in several research works \[[18](#9789815274349-C8.html#r18), [19](#9789815274349-C8.html#r19)\]. To enable farmers to choose wisely based on a prepared decision table, they separated the supervised field into tiny sectors and examined the information gathered by the sensing nodes sector-wise. These investigations demonstrated effectiveness in terms of data compression, energy preservation, and precise judgment. Agriculture is important for long-term financial growth and organizational change in terms of the preparation, creation, and transportation of domesticated animals and crops. At the moment, cutting-edge IoT, WiFi, machine learning, and AI technologies \[[20](#9789815274349-C8.html#r20)\] are having an impact on the agriculture sector. With the aid of these technologies, data collection and analysis on factors like temperature, weather, soil characteristics, and previous crop performance can now be used to predict future crop performance and address issues with weed management, crop diseases, pesticide control, and water management \[[21](#9789815274349-C8.html#r21), [22](#9789815274349-C8.html#r22)\]. An artificial neural network-based (ANN) model is proposed in a study \[[23](#9789815274349-C8.html#r23)\] to predict the formation of frost using parallel reasoning. Another model \[[24](#9789815274349-C8.html#r24)\] distinguishes cotton weeds from cotton plants and soil with an accuracy of 92%. In another study \[[25](#9789815274349-C8.html#r25)\], the ANN model for predicting water resource variables and the issues to be considered while developing the learning models are presented. In a research work \[[26](#9789815274349-C8.html#r26)\], a model based on ANN and an expert system to identify crop nutritional disorders is proposed. A model to monitor soil moisture using a transceiver equipped with UAV is proposed in a study \[[27](#9789815274349-C8.html#r27)\]

An expert system using fuzzy logic for the precision agriculture of soya beans \[[28](#9789815274349-C8.html#r28)\] enables farmers to get the correct assistance from an agricultural expert in time. Another fuzzy-based model \[[29](#9789815274349-C8.html#r29)\] is implemented to grade the leaf disease automatically by segmenting the color image and computing the total leaf area and illness area. An inference system using fuzzy logic is created \[[30](#9789815274349-C8.html#r30)\] to predict plant diseases based on weather data. The “IF, THEN” condition is used to signal diseases only when the measured temperature and humidity value falls within a specific range. An IOT-based expert system helps to increase productivity \[[31](#9789815274349-C8.html#r31)\] and reduce the loss due to disease by making informed decisions.

Due to the lack of readily accessible trustworthy information on the origins of food goods, the current systems also lack customer trust \[[32](#9789815274349-C8.html#r32)\]. Consequently, blockchain technology is a helpful tool for offering a practical fix to the aforementioned problems. Since every node (user) linked to the blockchain has a replica of every transaction, these details kept on a blockchain cannot be changed or deleted. As a result, both the transaction and validity may be independently checked. Numerous existing technologies for data collection, storage, and distribution are integrated with blockchain technology \[[33](#9789815274349-C8.html#r33)\]. The autonomous nature allows the nodes in the network to be homologous and not bound to the network \[[34](#9789815274349-C8.html#r34)\]. Blockchain enables the reading, validating, and storing of a shared ledger of transactions in a chain of blocks \[[35](#9789815274349-C8.html#r35)\]. Smart agriculture systems, which are more efficient than e-agriculture systems, include sensor technology like GPS and IoT, cloud computing, and mobile technologies \[[36](#9789815274349-C8.html#r36)\]. Additionally, IoT-enabled farm management systems are easily intercepted by hackers, who can then alter the data. The key characteristics of blockchain technology can address e-agricultural systems' operational issues for smart agriculture \[[37](#9789815274349-C8.html#r37)\]. The food supply networks and agricultural productivity have significantly improved thanks to the agricultural systems. Many scholars and organizations are considering agriculture 4.0 \[[38](#9789815274349-C8.html#r38)^(\-)[40](#9789815274349-C8.html#r40)\], which incorporates big data, blockchain, IoT, and precision agriculture for all facets of sustainable agriculture. In order to preserve sustainable agricultural practices and promote healthy crop growth, farmers can use these technologies to observe and analyze the data.

According to Zhang \[[33](#9789815274349-C8.html#r33)\], blockchain technology in agriculture could streamline the credit-checking and financing process. To realize its full potential, blockchain technology must be incorporated into the already-existing legacy systems and IoT-based systems. Its adoption is constrained by technological obstacles such as infrastructure management, scalability, interoperability, and excessive power consumption. However, blockchain has the potential to be utilized for financial transactions, government-to-business and business-to-business transactions, as well as secure machine-to-machine communication between IoT devices, enabling smart and sustainable agriculture even on a big scale.

## TECHNOLOGIES USED

Emerging technologies like Artificial Intelligence (AI), blockchain, drones, the Internet of Things (IoT), and big data analytics can raise productivity and effectiveness at all points in the agricultural value chain \[[41](#9789815274349-C8.html#r41)^(\-)[46](#9789815274349-C8.html#r46)\]. This can increase farmers' incomes, increase farm productivity while decreasing waste, and improve supply-chain efficiency, transparency, and sustainable resource use.

### IoT in Precision Agriculture

IoT devices and sensors play a significant role in precision agriculture by gathering data in real time on numerous aspects of crop development and environmental conditions \[[47](#9789815274349-C8.html#r47), [48](#9789815274349-C8.html#r48)\]. To make educated judgments about irrigation, fertilization, pest management, and other agricultural practices, the data gathered by these devices and sensors is analyzed. Sample examples of the various IoT gadgets and sensors employed in precision agriculture are discussed below.

#### Detectors of Soil Moisture

By measuring soil moisture levels, these sensors help farmers optimize irrigation and avoid overwatering \[[49](#9789815274349-C8.html#r49), [50](#9789815274349-C8.html#r50)\]. To establish the most effective irrigation plan, they can either be embedded directly in the soil or used in conjunction with weather sensors \[[51](#9789815274349-C8.html#r51)\]. Fig. ([**1**](#9789815274349-C8.html#f1)) shows the soil moisture sensor.

#### Weather Monitoring Sensor

Dynamic variation of environmental conditions is a major factor influencing the accuracy of sensor data \[[53](#9789815274349-C8.html#r53)\]. Sensor nodes deployed in the crop field are vulnerable to dynamic environments, like rainfall, temperature instabilities, variations in wind speed, daylight, and other aspects. A major open challenge that calls for the research community to put in significant effort is the advance of new approaches for atmospheric modification, cloud discovery, and sound interpolation. It can gauge temperature, humidity, wind speed, and other elements of the environment that may have an impact on crop growth. Farmers can modify their farming practices by gathering information on these issues.

![](Images/9789815274349-C8-F1.jpg)**Fig. (1))**  
Soil Moisture Sensor \[[52](#9789815274349-C8.html#r52)\]. 

#### Sensors for Crop Health

DHT11 and LDR, along with camera and soil moisture sensors \[[54](#9789815274349-C8.html#r54)\], can measure a plant's health by looking for indications of disease, pest, and nutritional shortage. This enables farmers to identify difficulties early and take appropriate action before they worsen.

#### GPS Trackers

Precision planting is one of the main advantages of GPS tracking in agriculture \[[55](#9789815274349-C8.html#r55)\]. Fig. ([**2**](#9789815274349-C8.html#f2)) shows the GPS tracker. Fields can be mapped out using GPS monitoring technology, enabling farmers to sow crops precisely and effectively. Crop yields may increase as a result of more effective use of resources like water and fertilizer. It enables farmers to optimize their processes and increase efficiency by tracking the movement of livestock and farm equipment.

#### Drone

Small, autonomous aircraft, known as drones, are employed in agriculture to support field management, precision farming, and crop monitoring \[[56](#9789815274349-C8.html#r56)\]. On crop health, soil quality, irrigation, fertilizer, and pest management, they can offer real-time data \[[57](#9789815274349-C8.html#r57)^(\-)[59](#9789815274349-C8.html#r59)\]. They can also cut down on operating expenses, pollution, and labor costs. Farmers are using drones more frequently as they provide a more effective and economical means of managing crops. Fig. (**3**) illustrates the usage of drones in field monitoring.

![](Images/9789815274349-C8-F2.jpg)**Fig. (2))**  
GPS Trackers \[[60](#9789815274349-C8.html#r60)\]. ![](Images/9789815274349-C8-F3.jpg)**Fig. (3))**  
Drone \[[61](#9789815274349-C8.html#r61)\]. 

### Role of AI in Precision Agriculture

Precision agriculture benefits greatly from artificial intelligence (AI), which gives farmers accurate, up-to-date information on their crops and soil conditions. To offer information on crop growth, nutritional levels, and insect infestations, AI-powered technologies may analyze data from sensors, drones, and satellites. This knowledge aids farmers in making data-driven decisions about insect control, fertilization, and irrigation that increase yields and decrease waste. Additionally, AI can help predict weather patterns, which enables farmers to modify their planting and harvesting schedules as necessary. Overall, AI in precision agriculture can assist farmers in streamlining their processes, minimizing their negative effects on the environment, and boosting profitability \[[62](#9789815274349-C8.html#r62)^(\-)[64](#9789815274349-C8.html#r64)\]. India must confront risks like supply chain disruptions, pandemics, and climate change in addition to the issue of feeding a growing population. By encouraging the application of artificial intelligence and other technologies, the Indian agricultural industry is revolutionized. The technology is being used by almost 7,000 farmers to examine the soil and monitor crop quality and health.

### Role of Blockchain in Precision Agriculture

Blockchain technology can be utilized to gather data about the quality of seed, supervise crop growth, and trace a crop's path after it leaves the farm. If blockchains are properly validated and put into use, they can stop unethical and unlawful production and distribution that threatens sustainability and local food security \[[65](#9789815274349-C8.html#r65)^(\-)[67](#9789815274349-C8.html#r67)\]. In order to ensure user trust, traditional blockchains are decentralized and democratized. Small-scale farmers who lack the necessary size, scale, and technological know-how to take part may also be excluded if corporations control the knowledge about the supply chain. Many academics contend that both large and small farms are necessary to sustain the world's expanding population and that the gap between large and small-scale food manufacturers can contribute to global food uncertainty. A number of issues need to be addressed before blockchain and other information technologies can help with food security. Blockchain adoption has to be distributed to include small farmers and rural residents \[[68](#9789815274349-C8.html#r68)\]. As a result, consumers will be able to make educated decisions, and sustainable and equitable food systems will be enabled. However, given the added responsibility that blockchains place on the end user, issues like low digital literacy among the world's poor and infrastructure limitations can break true decentralization. In order to make them sensitive to social and environmental values necessary to address food insecurity among various groups, they must also be incorporated into larger food security promotion efforts. By combining the Internet of Things and blockchain technology, smart agriculture can take the form of a network of smart farms with increasing influence over supply chains \[[69](#9789815274349-C8.html#r69)\]. This combination will produce a greater degree of sovereignty and cleverness in the management of precision agriculture in ways that are more effective and optimized.

## PROPOSED MODEL

In this chapter, we propose a model based on IoT, AI, and blockchain for smart access to government schemes by farmers. The various stages in the construction of the proposed model are discussed in this section.

### Drone Monitoring System (DMS)

Drones are considered the most promising technology that has revolutionized the various operations involved in agriculture \[[70](#9789815274349-C8.html#r70)\]. Drones are otherwise known as Unmanned Aerial Vehicles (UAVs). These Smaller UAVs with greater cargo capacity are now conceivable because of advancements in embedded systems and motor technology. Drones may be launched almost instantaneously and can help with remote coverage of the affected areas without the need for operations or safety planning. The aerial view of the crops affected by natural calamities like floods, storms, and bad weather can be observed with the help of drone monitoring systems. Using drones, images of the affected crops are collected, providing spectral information in the infrared and visual ranges. The affected fields can be efficiently explored and the environmental impact can be assessed using DMS. Because of the drone, the images of the affected field can be collected from the desired location with the desired height at the desired frequency. This helps in making the assessment more precise. Fig. ([**4**](#9789815274349-C8.html#f4)) shows the components of the drone monitoring system. The DMS is empowered by incorporating the power of an artificial intelligence-based machine learning model. The spatial variability in the field due to calamities can be assessed with the help of a trained learning model using the images captured by drones.

![](Images/9789815274349-C8-F4.jpg)**Fig. (4))**  
Drone monitoring system. 

#### Soil Health Monitoring System

Soil health is monitored remotely using sophisticated image processing technology along with the UAV loaded with remote sensing cameras. The information required for assessing the health of the soil is collected by measuring the electromagnetic spectrum of light that is reflected from the surface soil. Scaled maps of the conditions across wide expanses of farmland are produced using multispectral photography. The elements present in the soil are different from each other in how they reflect wavelengths of light. Multispectral imaging sensors are used for collecting piles of data about these wavelengths. The gathered information is fed into a machine-learning model for analysis. Using the digital mapping of soil classification, the physical and chemical properties of soil can be assessed efficiently. These findings are recorded as a chain of blocks in encrypted form along with time spatial details. Fig. ([**5**](#9789815274349-C8.html#f5)) illustrates the process of creating blocks of information required for analyzing the soil health. The DMS takes the spatiotemporal data from a chain of blocks and compares the impact of calamities on the health of the soil. If the impact is above the threshold level that can be fixed by a human expert from the authorized agency, then the farmer of the corresponding field is considered to be eligible for the scheme. A new block with this identified scheme details, along with the farmer ID, is created and added to the blockchain of farmer details.

![](Images/9789815274349-C8-F5.jpg)**Fig. (5))**  
Creation of Block – Soil Health. 

#### Crop Health Monitoring System

We propose to measure the impact of natural calamities on the health of crops using the stream of images taken from drones. The first vital factor to be assessed is a rise in the water level due to rain and flood. Next, the impact of calamities on the health of crops is analyzed using the same multispectral image data collected from the drone and AI-based classification models.

To measure the depth of water in the field due to flood, a drone mounted with ultrasonic sensors is flown over the field to be monitored. The distance between the sensor and the field surface is measured using the following equation.

| ![](Images/9789815274349-C8-EQ1.jpg) || (1) |
| --- | --- |

Where ‘d’ is the distance of the object, ‘t’ is the time taken for the sound wave to reach the receiver, and ‘s’ is the speed of the sound, which is 343 meters per second. This stream of data (d[i], l[i]) coming out of the sensor along with spatial information (l[i]) is fed into the trained model. The training model extracts information related to the depth of water level and crop health using the data received from the sensors. This is illustrated in Fig. ([**6**](#9789815274349-C8.html#f6)).

![](Images/9789815274349-C8-F6.jpg)**Fig. (6))**  
Creation of block - crop health. 

### Health Assessing Model

The information used for analyzing the health of soil and crops is available as a sequence of blocks in the blockchain BC\_DMS(Blockchain for DMS). The health assessing model is fed with information about the soil and crop along with the details of calamities. The impact on the crop’s health due to flood is assessed based on the level of water information and duration of water logging and calamities detail. The average height of the crop is measured by comparing the distance between the crop and the water, which is sensed by the ultrasonic sensor. If the distance is less than the threshold value, the impact level is classified based on the water level. The multispectral image is analyzed by the training model, and the health status of the crop is classified. A convolutional neural network-based learning model with two hidden layers is considered to be the best model for the proposed scheme.

### Blockchains for Smart Access to Government Schemes

To enable farmers to access the eligible schemes in time with no delay, the information associated with the health status of soil and crops is maintained in a blockchain called BC\_DMS. To track the status of scheme utilization by eligible farmers, a separate blockchain called BC\_FSS (BC for Field-wise Sanctioned schemes) is maintained.

#### Blockchain for DMS (BC\_DMS)

The details related to the health status of soil and crops are precisely identified using the machine learning model from the images gathered by the drones at the desired height at desired locations. The identified features are grouped in blocks along with time, location, and FID (Field ID). This block is added to the chain of BC\_DMS.

#### BC for Field-wise Sanctioned Schemes (BC\_FSS)

Based on the details available in the BC\_DMS, the health status of soil and crop is assessed using AI-based trained machine model. The models identify the suitable schemes for which the farmer is an eligible beneficiary. A block that carries the information related to the eligible schemes, scheme details, and the corresponding FID is created. This block is chained with the BC\_FSS blockchain. The construction process of the BC\_FSS blockchain is illustrated in Fig. (**7**).

![](Images/9789815274349-C8-F7.jpg)**Fig. (7))**  
Construction of BC\_FSS blockchain 

#### Smart Contracts

The main objective of the proposed framework is to show the usage of blockchain in tracing the fund allocation to eligible farmers based on the schemes identified and stored in the BC\_FSS. It also aims to monitor the farmer's utilization of the allocated funds. This is implemented using the smart contract written in the programming language called Solidity and the work is based on using Ethereum-based blockchain technology. The proposed framework can be implemented using the Remix development environment. For tracing the fund allocation and utilization, the contract makes use of attributes, operational functions, and events. The contract, named “sanction amount verification”, is proposed with the required attributes, functions, and events, as shown in Fig. ([**8**](#9789815274349-C8.html#f8)). It verifies whether the amount transferred to the farmer’s account is the same as the amount sanctioned using the function Transaction\_checking (). The function uses the details of sanctioned schemes like the amount, farmers’ account number, scheme, *etc.*, which are available in the BC\_FSS. The authenticity of the transaction is verified using the contract function called InitateTrancation().I It ensures whether the amount is transferred from the authorized office and received by the farmer to whom the amount is sanctioned. Another contract called utilization checker is used for verifying and tracing whether the sanctioned amount is utilized properly by the farmer for the development of the crop field. The required attributes, functions, and events are given in Fig. ([**9**](#9789815274349-C8.html#f9)). Once utilization details are uploaded by the farmer, the verification () function checks the authenticity and transfers this to the approval process. The approval function validates the details furnished by the farmer by analyzing the health status of the field received from the field sensor. The overall architecture of the proposed model is shown in Fig. ([**10**](#9789815274349-C8.html#f10)). Once the schemes are identified based on the level of impact on the crops and soil due to calamities, the farmers and agents can access the details from the blockchain. The enciphered nature of blockchain prevents the unauthorized modification of details related to the health status of crops and approved schemes. Also, it ensures transparency in the sanctioning of the government schemes and allows the farmers to taste the benefits of appropriate schemes in time.

![](Images/9789815274349-C8-F8.jpg)**Fig. (8))**  
Amount sanction verification contract. ![](Images/9789815274349-C8-F9.jpg)**Fig. (9))**  
Amount sanction verification contract. ![](Images/9789815274349-C8-F10.jpg)**Fig. (10))**  
Architecture of the Smart Access Model for Government Schemes. 

## CONCLUSION

The government has taken a lot of initiatives and announced various schemes for the welfare of farmers who are the backbone of most developing countries. Since most of the farmers are living in remote rural areas, the schemes announced by the government, especially during natural calamities like drought, flood, and cyclones, do not reach the farmers in time. Our proposed model automatically and precisely measures the level of impact on crops and soil due to natural calamities with the help of IoT-enabled machine-learning models. It identifies the schemes based on the level of impact and sanctions of the scheme. This enables the farmers to enjoy the benefits of government schemes without delay. In addition, since the level of impact is analyzed by the machines with less intervention of humans, human-made mistakes and corruption are minimized. Also, all necessary transactions related to the schemes are automatized and maintained as a block in a blockchain without the intervention dealers. This minimizes the possible crimes related to the involvement of intermediated dealers between the government and farme**rs.**

# Transforming Agriculture with IoT for Precision Agriculture and Sustainable Crop Management

  
  
**Suyash Bhardwaj^([1](#9789815274349-C9.html#aff1), [\*](#9789815274349-C9.html#cor1))****,** **Sasirekha Venkatesan^([2](#9789815274349-C9.html#aff2))****,** **Swati Rawat^([3](#9789815274349-C9.html#aff3))****,** **Pashupati Nath^([4](#9789815274349-C9.html#aff4))**  
¹ Department of Computer Science Engineering, Gurukula Kangri Deemed to be University, Uttarakhand, India  
² Department of Management Studies, Sri Sairam Engineering College, Chennai, India  
³ MMICT&BM, Maharishi Markandeshwar Deemed to be University, Haryana, India  
⁴ College of Smart Agriculture, COER University, Uttarakhand, India  
  

## Abstract

The Internet of Things (IoT) technology is making a radical transition in the agricultural business, resulting in the creation of precision agriculture and sustainable crop management practices. This study inspects how Internet of Things (IoT) technology is revolutionizing agriculture, with a particular emphasis on sustainable crop management techniques and precision agriculture. The study explores the extent and significance of using sensors, IoT devices, and data analytics for improved crop monitoring and management, empowering farmers to make data-driven choices. Farmers are able to allocate resources more efficiently and produce less waste due to the real-time data collecting on soil moisture, temperature, humidity, and crop health. We go into great detail on the essential elements of IoT-based precision agriculture, such as decision support systems, data collecting, analytics, and sensor technology. The study also looks at the benefits of using IoT in agriculture, highlighting how technology might completely transform farming methods for more sustainability and efficiency. A thorough literature study adds to our understanding of the status of research in Internet of Things applications for sustainable crop management and precision agriculture.

**Keywords:** Artificial intelligence (AI), Internet of things (IoT), IoT in agriculture, Precision agriculture, Sustainable crop management.

## INTRODUCTION TO IOT IN AGRICULTURE

The interconnection of physical things, automobiles, structures, and other items that are merged with software, sensors, electronics, and connections to networks, allowing them to collect and transmit data, is referred to as the Internet of Things

(IoT). The purpose of IoT is to build a seamless communication network between items and systems, allowing the development of smart arrangements capable of collecting, treating, and analyzing data in order to make choices and conduct actions \[[1](#9789815274349-C9.html#r1), [2](#9789815274349-C9.html#r2)\].

The Internet of Things (IoT) idea goes back to the late 1980s when academics first began investigating the possibility of linking common things to the internet. However, the technology and infrastructure required to execute IoT did not become accessible until the 2000s. IoT has become a fast-developing industry, with applications in a variety of businesses, such as agriculture, as a result of broad internet usage and the growth of low-cost devices and wireless communication technologies \[[3](#9789815274349-C9.html#r3)\].

The usage of IoT in agriculture has developed quickly in the past few years as farmers seek ways to enhance efficiency, lower costs, and raise yields. IoT-based agriculture systems have been utilized to automate operations such as crop and soil health monitoring, irrigation system control, and animal tracking. IoT in agriculture has also enabled the assembly and examination of vast sizes of data, giving farmers useful insights into their operations \[[4](#9789815274349-C9.html#r4)\]. The application of IoT technology and systems to improve agricultural practices and boost efficiency is referred to as IoT in agriculture. This may be accomplished by collecting data on soil health, crop growth, weather patterns, and other elements that affect agricultural operations using sensors, cloud computing, and other IoT-based skills. This data may then be evaluated to help farmers make better decisions and improve agricultural practices, resulting in higher yields, lower costs, and a more sustainable agriculture business \[[5](#9789815274349-C9.html#r5), [6](#9789815274349-C9.html#r6)\].

This research attempts to highlight the most recent trends and scientific findings in the fields of IoT and agriculture. According to Google Trends, there has been a significant increase in searches for the terms precision agriculture and sustainable crop management. Precision agriculture and sustainable crop management have had varying degrees of popularity. The chart below depicts the web search prevalence for the aforementioned terms as determined by Google search trends since 2024.

This chapter provides a widespread outline of the Internet of Things (IoT) in agriculture. The chapter begins with an introduction to IoT, outlining the concept and underlining its many agricultural applications. The chapter goes on to discuss the advantages of installing IoT-based agricultural systems. It then looks into the many types of IoT sensors used in agricultural, including soil moisture sensors, crop sensors, weather sensors, animal sensors, and irrigation sensors. The sections that follow go into further depth on IoT-based data collection and management systems, covering both centralized and decentralized approaches. It talks about cloud-based data management systems as well as the emerging trend of edge computing in IoT-based agriculture, underlining the need of real-time data processing and localized decision making. The chapter focuses on IoT-based precision agriculture, providing both an explanation and a summary of the advantages of employing precision agricultural practices. The chapter then goes into IoT-based sustainable agriculture management, such as crop monitoring, automated irrigation systems, and pest and disease management using IoT technologies. Finally, the chapter reviews the key findings and examines the importance of IoT in transforming agriculture into more efficient, sustainable, and technologically driven agricultural practices. The references section includes a comprehensive list of all sources and publications cited throughout the chapter, making it an invaluable resource for agricultural researchers, practitioners, and policymakers interested in the possibilities of IoT.

### Definition of IoT

The Internet of Things (IoT) is a collection of physical objects, including automobiles, buildings, and other items, that are connected to the internet and equipped with software, hardware, sensors, and network connections. The purpose of IoT is to build a seamless communication network between items and systems, allowing the development of smart organizations capable of collecting, processing, and analyzing data in order to make choices and conduct actions \[[7](#9789815274349-C9.html#r7), [8](#9789815274349-C9.html#r8)\]. The Internet of Things (IoT) is the convergence of a number of technologies, including artificial intelligence (AI), ubiquitous computing, inexpensive sensors, and increasingly potent embedded systems \[[9](#9789815274349-C9.html#r9)\]. Several instances include healthcare delivery systems, cameras, fixtures for lighting, heating and cooling systems, security systems for homes, and other products \[[10](#9789815274349-C9.html#r10)\].

### Applications of IoT in Agriculture

According to current estimates, 9.6 billion people will populate the world by 2050. In order to feed this massive population, agriculture is being compelled to use the Internet of Things. IoT is eradicating concerns such as harsh weather, climatic shifts, and environmental degradation while also supporting us in addressing the growing need for food \[[11](#9789815274349-C9.html#r11)\]. Previously, mechanical innovations such as tractors were introduced for managing the ever-growing food demand. Many more sophisticated sensors are being used in agriculture as a result of the development of industrial IoT. A mobile or satellite communication network currently connects the sensors to the cloud. It enables us to get sensor data instantly and make defensible decisions.

IoT tenders in the agriculture industry have significantly improved irrigation systems by enabling real-time monitoring of water tank echelons. The widespread use of sensors throughout the agricultural process has allowed for precise tracking of crop development and resource utilization \[[12](#9789815274349-C9.html#r12), [13](#9789815274349-C9.html#r13)\]. The benefits of IoT in agriculture are evident, as it has facilitated effective water utilization, optimized inputs, and revolutionized farming practices. IoT-based smart farming \[[14](#9789815274349-C9.html#r14)\] continually monitors the land, reducing excessive resource usage and saving farmers time through sensor-based adjustments in humidity, temperature, and soil conditions. Data collection on rainfall, humidity, temperature, infestations of insects, and composition of the soil are just a few of the many IoT uses in agriculture. This data enables automation, informed decision-making, waste reduction, risk management, and efficient crop management \[[15](#9789815274349-C9.html#r15), [16](#9789815274349-C9.html#r16)\]. Farmers can remotely monitor soil conditions and utilize IoT data to create precise fertilization plans, enhancing the overall agricultural system.

The ultimate objective is to integrate information from sensors with the farmers' understanding and instinct about his or her private property in order to boost agricultural output while lowering expenses. There are several IoT applications in agriculture, but just a handful are shown in Fig. ([**1**](#9789815274349-C9.html#f1)), with a brief summary of each application.

![](Images/9789815274349-C9-F1.jpg)**Fig. (1))**  
Google Trends reaction for the terms “Precision Agriculture” and “Sustainable Crop Management” since 2004. ![](Images/9789815274349-C9-F2.jpg)**Fig. (2))**  
Application of IoT in agriculture. 

Some of the applications of IoT in agriculture are as follows:

#### Precision agriculture

Utilizing innovations like the Internet of Things (IoT)-based sensors for precise monitoring and control, precision agriculture applies science and technology to manage spatial and temporal variability in agricultural output. This allows farmers to increase yields and make well-informed decisions while maintaining environmental sustainability \[[17](#9789815274349-C9.html#r17)\].

#### Agricultural Drones

In comparison to traditional land vehicles, drones cover areas that are ten to fifteen times larger and come with computer-controlled automation for tasks like weather investigation, crop disease monitoring, and soil quality assessment. These drones are revolutionizing industries like agriculture and improving crop management \[[18](#9789815274349-C9.html#r18), [19](#9789815274349-C9.html#r19)\].

#### Automated irrigation

Precision agriculture reduces water waste and increases crop yields using automated irrigation systems that use Internet of Things (IoT) sensors to monitor soil humidity, weather, and environmental variables. These systems optimize water distribution to crops based on real-time data. Numerous varieties, including pneumatic, portable timer, hybrid timer/sensor, SCADA, and paddock control modules, provide a range of methods for effectively managing irrigation, remotely modifying structures, and even regulating pumps and motors to enhance water resource conservation \[[20](#9789815274349-C9.html#r20), [21](#9789815274349-C9.html#r21)\].

#### Livestock Monitoring

Using temperature, weight, and activity sensors, an Internet of Things-enabled livestock monitoring system tracks and manages sheep, pigs, chickens, and cattle. It analyzes data to optimize care, keep an eye on health and behavior, and automate feeding and watering schedules in an effort to improve animal productivity, efficiency, and well-being in general \[[22](#9789815274349-C9.html#r22)\].

#### Crop Monitoring System

The IoT-based crop monitoring system offers crucial insights into crop growth and enables scientific advice and preventative steps in the face of fluctuating global weather conditions \[[23](#9789815274349-C9.html#r23), [24](#9789815274349-C9.html#r24)\]. Through an internet-connected sensor network, an agricultural monitoring system gathers and analyses data to optimize practices like irrigation, fertilization, and pest control, with the aim of improving crop yield, efficiency, and sustainability. It does this by using IoT sensors for temperature, moisture, and light \[[25](#9789815274349-C9.html#r25), [26](#9789815274349-C9.html#r26)\].

#### Smart Greenhouses

Smart greenhouses, powered by IoT, automate and optimize environmental factors for precise crop management, reducing water use by up to 70%. Remote monitoring enhances efficiency, preventing diseases, and minimizing energy consumption \[[27](#9789815274349-C9.html#r27), [28](#9789815274349-C9.html#r28)\]. Proper greenhouse design and orientation, emphasizing energy efficiency, contribute to uniform crop quality and increased nutritional value \[[29](#9789815274349-C9.html#r29), [30](#9789815274349-C9.html#r30)\].

#### Environmental Monitoring

IoT-based real-time environment tracking uses sensors for humidity, temperature, light, and soil moisture, enabling efficient data collection and remote monitoring. This technology benefits agriculture and other industries, optimizing output, conserving resources, and improving efficiency and profitability \[[31](#9789815274349-C9.html#r31)\].

#### Remote Sensing

IoT-based remote sensing with strategically placed devices monitors crops in real time, aiding farmers in taking prompt action to prevent diseases, track crop development, and make informed decisions on weather patterns and soil quality for precise irrigation and cultivation \[[32](#9789815274349-C9.html#r32), [33](#9789815274349-C9.html#r33)\].

#### Supply Chain Management

IoT in agriculture utilizes monitoring devices for an efficient supply chain, reducing waste. Farmers benefit from valuable data, enabling informed decisions, automation, and increased productivity, fostering sustainability \[[34](#9789815274349-C9.html#r34)\].

### Advantages of IoT-based agriculture systems

IoT-based agriculture systems provide a number of benefits that can increase agricultural operations' efficiency, profitability, and sustainability \[[35](#9789815274349-C9.html#r35)\]. Among the primary advantages are:

***Improved Efficiency***: IoT-based solutions automate processes like crop and soil health monitoring, irrigation system control, and animal tracking, decreasing the need for manual labor and enhancing efficiency.

***Enhanced Yields***: IoT-based agriculture systems give farmers useful insights into their operations by collecting and analyzing data on soil health, crop development, and other aspects, allowing them to make educated decisions and enhance yields.

***Cost Savings***: IoT-based technologies automate operations, eliminating the need for manual labor and boosting efficiency, resulting in cost savings for farmers.

***Increased Profitability***: IoT-based agriculture solutions can boost farmers' profits by increasing yields and lowering expenses.

***Improved Sustainability***: IoT-based solutions may be utilized to decrease waste, preserve resources, and improve agricultural enterprises' sustainability.

***Better Decision-Making***: IoT-based systems gather and analyze vast volumes of data, giving farmers important insights into their operations and helping them to make informed decisions and enhance efficiency.

IoT-based solutions enable real-time tracking and monitoring of crops, soil health, animals, and other aspects, allowing farmers to respond rapidly to any changes or difficulties.

![](Images/9789815274349-C9-F3.jpg)**Fig. (3))**  
Advantages of IoT-based agriculture systems. 

IoT-based agriculture systems provide a number of benefits that can increase agricultural operations' efficiency, profitability, and sustainability \[[12](#9789815274349-C9.html#r12), [35](#9789815274349-C9.html#r35)\]. IoT-based technologies are revolutionizing the agriculture business by automating jobs, gathering and analyzing data, and delivering important insights into agricultural operations.

## IOT SENSORS IN AGRICULTURE

IoT sensors are devices that collect data on a variety of agricultural activities, including soil health, crop growth, weather patterns, and livestock health \[[36](#9789815274349-C9.html#r36)\]. The goal of IoT sensors in agriculture is to provide farmers with meaningful data and insights into their processes, enabling them to make informed decisions and improve the productivity, sustainability, and efficiency of their operations.

### Types of IoT sensors used in agriculture

Many IoT sensors are utilized in agriculture to gather information on a variety of agricultural processes, including soil quality, crop development, weather patterns, and livestock health. Here are some examples of common Internet of Things (IoT) sensors used in agriculture, along with an approximation of their costs, features, and applications.:

#### Soil Moisture Sensors

Soil moisture sensors detect soil moisture and temperature, supporting irrigation management and crop monitoring to prevent over- and under-watering. These electronic tools are essential for farmers and gardeners, ensuring the right water supply for crops and preventing crop failure when integrated with irrigation systems. Common types include capacitance, tensiometer, and temporal domain reflectometry (TDR) sensors \[[37](#9789815274349-C9.html#r37)\]. Capacitance sensors detect soil moisture by gauging changes in dielectric constant, tensiometers measure soil humidity tension, and TDR sensors assess moisture through electrical pulse travel time \[[37](#9789815274349-C9.html#r37), [38](#9789815274349-C9.html#r38)\]. Used in horticulture, agriculture, landscaping, and environmental monitoring, these sensors enhance productivity, prevent overwatering, reduce water waste, and improve irrigation efficiency, also measuring soil temperature, salinity, and nutrient content \[[38](#9789815274349-C9.html#r38), [39](#9789815274349-C9.html#r39)\].

![](Images/9789815274349-C9-F4.jpg)**Fig. (4))**  
Soil Moisture Sensor. 

#### Crop Sensors

Crop sensors capture data on crop health, growth, and metrics like nitrogen content, aiding informed agricultural decision-making and enabling timely action for stress or disease prevention \[[40](#9789815274349-C9.html#r40), [41](#9789815274349-C9.html#r41)\]. Active and passive models emit or monitor radiation, generating maps for precise input applications in agriculture, reducing costs, increasing yields, and minimizing environmental impact \[[41](#9789815274349-C9.html#r41)\].

![](Images/9789815274349-C9-F5.jpg)**Fig. (5))**  
Crop sensor. 

#### Weather Sensors

Weather sensors monitor temperature, humidity, and wind speed, providing essential data for precision agriculture \[[42](#9789815274349-C9.html#r42), [43](#9789815274349-C9.html#r43)\]. Installed on farms, these devices optimize irrigation, predict weather patterns, and conserve resources. Integrated into central networks, they offer real-time data access and remote monitoring, that is especially beneficial for farmers with remote locations or multiple farms \[[43](#9789815274349-C9.html#r43), [44](#9789815274349-C9.html#r44)\]. Various sensor types, including thermometers, barometers, anemometers, hygrometers, and rain gauges, can be used individually or combined for comprehensive environmental data, often integrated with GPS for more precise information \[[44](#9789815274349-C9.html#r44)\].

#### Livestock Sensors

Livestock sensors optimize cattle welfare by gathering data on health, behavior, and productivity, reducing disease risks \[[45](#9789815274349-C9.html#r45), [46](#9789815274349-C9.html#r46)\]. Continuously monitoring aspects such as body temperature, activity levels, feeding patterns, and reproduction rate, these devices integrated into central networks enable remote monitoring, improving productivity and animal welfare while reducing medication usage.

![](Images/9789815274349-C9-F6.jpg)**Fig. (6))**  
Weather sensors. ![](Images/9789815274349-C9-F6.jpg)**Fig. (6))**  
Weather sensors. ![](Images/9789815274349-C9-F7.jpg)**Fig. (7))**  
Livestock sensor. 

#### Irrigation Sensors

Irrigation sensors optimize water usage and enhance agricultural output by measuring soil moisture levels and connecting to irrigation systems for remote control and water conservation \[[47](#9789815274349-C9.html#r47)\]. As crucial components of IoT-based farming systems, these devices provide essential data and insights, enabling farmers to improve efficiency, profitability, and sustainability while conserving resources \[[48](#9789815274349-C9.html#r48)\]. The market provides a number of irrigation sensor types, each of which is designed to evaluate a certain environmental parameter. The following are the most common types of irrigation sensors:

***Soil moisture sensors*:**These sensors allow farmers to change irrigation schedules by determining how much water is available in the soil. They can be positioned at different soil depths to provide more exact information on the soil's moisture levels at different depths.

***Weather sensors*:**They are utilized to measure environmental variables, including hotness and moistness, wind speed, and solar radiation. Farmers can use them to anticipate weather trends and make adjustments to irrigation plans as needed \[[49](#9789815274349-C9.html#r49)\].

***Flow sensors***: These devices monitor the flow of water *via* an irrigation system, allowing farmers to detect leaks and ensure that the water is distributed consistently over the field. By measuring the volume of water lost by evaporation and transpiration from the soil and plants, evapotranspiration sensors let farmers adjust irrigation schedules to fit the water demands of their crops.

Irrigation sensors can be linked to a central network that provides real-time data and allows farmers to remotely monitor their crops. Farmers who need to monitor crops in remote areas or who have large fields may find this particularly useful \[[48](#9789815274349-C9.html#r48), [49](#9789815274349-C9.html#r49)\]. Irrigation sensors can help farmers save water, protect the environment, and enhance agricultural production.

![](Images/9789815274349-C9-F8.jpg)**Fig. (8))**  
Irrigation sensor. 

### Sensor Technologies for Monitoring Crop Health

Crop health and condition monitoring is critical for optimizing agricultural practices and guaranteeing optimal production. Sensor technologies are essential for providing real-time crop health indicators data, allowing agriculturalists to make educated choices about irrigation, fertilization, pest management, and other treatments \[[50](#9789815274349-C9.html#r50), [51](#9789815274349-C9.html#r51)\]. A number of sensor technologies are used to monitor crop health, including:

#### Optical Sensors

These sensors use light to assess crop health, such as chlorophyll concentration, which is an important indication of crop health and yield.

#### Spectral Sensors

These sensors monitor crop spectrum reflectance, providing significant information about crop health and yield.

![](Images/9789815274349-C9-F9.jpg)**Fig. (9))**  
Using optical sensor for detecting plant health. ![](Images/9789815274349-C9-F10.jpg)**Fig. (10))**  
Multi Spectral Sensor \[[97](#9789815274349-C9.html#r97)\]. 

#### Multispectral and Hyperspectral Imaging

These sensors take pictures of crops using a mix of visible and near-infrared light, giving crucial data on crop health, such as plant stress and nutritional deficits.

![](Images/9789815274349-C9-F11.jpg)**Fig. (11))**  
Multispectral and hyperspectral imaging \[[96](#9789815274349-C9.html#r96)\]. 

#### Thermal Imaging

These sensors assess crop temperature, providing significant information about crop health and yield.

![](Images/9789815274349-C9-F12.jpg)**Fig. (12))**  
Thermal sensor in action. 

#### Drone-based Sensors

Drones using sensors, such as cameras and spectrometers, may capture airborne photos and data on crop health indicators, such as chlorophyll content and plant stress, and give vital data on crop health and production.

![](Images/9789815274349-C9-F13.jpg)**Fig. (13))**  
Drone-based sensor \[[98](#9789815274349-C9.html#r98)\]. 

#### Soil Sensors

These sensors assess soil health parameters such as moisture content, temperature, and nutrient levels, providing significant information on crop health and yield. Sensor technologies are important in crop health monitoring because they provide useful data and insights into the factors that affect crop development and overall agricultural operations. Farmers may upsurge the efficiency, effectiveness, and sustainability of their processes by selecting the correct sensors \[[52](#9789815274349-C9.html#r52)\].

![](Images/9789815274349-C9-F14.jpg)**Fig. (14))**  
Soil sensor. 

## IOT-BASED DATA COLLECTION AND MANAGEMENT

The emergence of the Internet of Things (IoT) has revolutionized the gathering, handling, and use of agricultural data. Real-time data retrieval from linked technologies and measuring equipment is made possible by IoT-based systems, which promotes better farming practices, more agricultural production, and informed decision-making. Gathering, managing, and analyzing data from several sources, such as sensors and weather stations, is the fundamental goal of IoT-based smart agricultural systems. This process yields insightful information that helps decision-makers make well-informed choices \[[53](#9789815274349-C9.html#r53)\]. The first stage of IoT-based data collecting is called a sensor network, which is made up of wireless sensors positioned throughout fields to collect data on temperature, light intensity, humidity, soil moisture, and nutrient levels. This allows for a complete picture of the agricultural environment.

### Overview of Data Collection and Management Systems

In IoT-based smart agriculture systems, data gathering and administration systems are crucial. These systems' purpose is to assemble data from a range of sources, including sensors, weather stations, and drones, and to store and analyze data in a way that delivers important insights and enables informed decision-making.

Data gathering and management systems are classified into two types: centralized and decentralized \[[54](#9789815274349-C9.html#r54)\].

#### Centralized Data Collection and Management Systems

A centralized system collects and stores all data in a single area, such as a cloud-based server. This allows all data to be processed and examined centralized, offering significant insights into the circumstances affecting crop development and overall agricultural operations.

#### Decentralized Data Collection and Management Systems

In a decentralized system, data is gathered and stored locally at edge devices like weather stations, drones, and sensors. This allows for real-time analysis and insights into the factors driving crop development and overall agricultural operations. The specific requirements of the agriculture industry determine which decentralized or centralized solution is best. Important elements of data gathering, administration, and IoT-based smart agricultural systems offer critical insights that help farmers improve long-term sustainability, productivity, and economic viability.

### Cloud-based Data Management Systems

Cloud-based solutions, which are widely used in agriculture, offer scalable infrastructure for managing Internet of Things data and come with benefits, including cost-effectiveness, scalability, accessibility, and sophisticated analytics \[[55](#9789815274349-C9.html#r55)\]. Farmers can obtain important insights into the variables influencing crop development and overall operations by processing, storing, and analyzing large amounts of data in an effective manner \[[55](#9789815274349-C9.html#r55)\]. These systems act as centralized repositories with infinite capacity, enabling simple access to data at any time and from any place \[[56](#9789815274349-C9.html#r56)\]. They provide cost-effective real-time deployment for maximizing output, profitability, and sustainability through data-driven decision-making, thanks to their scalable accessibility and strong security.

### Edge Computing in IoT-based Agriculture

Edge computing, a revolutionary approach for IoT-based agriculture, processes data near the source, improving latency and decision-making based on real-time data for farmers \[[57](#9789815274349-C9.html#r57)\]. By addressing issues like limited network capacity, connectivity challenges, and privacy concerns, this decentralized approach enhances overall efficiency, reliability, and security in remote or resource-constrained agricultural settings. In IoT-based agriculture, edge computing involves processing data from sensors, drones, and other devices at the network's edge, empowering farmers to boost efficiency, profitability, and sustainability \[[58](#9789815274349-C9.html#r58)\].

## IOT-BASED PRECISION AGRICULTURE

Precision agriculture, driven by the Internet of Things (IoT), optimizes practices and boosts production by collecting real-time data on climate, soil moisture, crop development, and insect infestations through IoT devices, sensors, and connectivity \[[16](#9789815274349-C9.html#r16), [17](#9789815274349-C9.html#r17)\]. Analyzed using powerful analytics and machine learning, this data provides actionable insights for precise decision-making, resulting in optimized resource allocation, reduced input waste, improved crop health monitoring, increased yields, and environmentally friendly farming practices. Transforming traditional techniques, IoT-based precision agriculture contributes to building a more productive and sustainable agricultural business \[[26](#9789815274349-C9.html#r26), [49](#9789815274349-C9.html#r49)\].

### Definition of Precision Agriculture

Precision agriculture is a data-driven agricultural technique that uses technology to optimize crop yield and reduce waste. Precision agriculture collects data on numerous elements of crop development and management, such as soil fertility, crop yield, and weather patterns, using a range of technologies such as sensors, GPS, drones, and other agricultural equipment. After gathering the information, it is analyzed to produce more informed crop management decisions., such as which seeds to plant, how much water to use, and when to apply fertilizers and pesticides. Precision agriculture aims to boost efficiency, improve yields, and minimize waste, ultimately leading to increased profitability for farmers and more environmental sustainability \[[16](#9789815274349-C9.html#r16), [17](#9789815274349-C9.html#r17), [59](#9789815274349-C9.html#r59)\].

### Advantages of Precision Agriculture

Precision agriculture has various advantages over traditional farming methods, including:

***Enhanced efficiency:*** Precision agriculture helps farmers make more educated crop management decisions, resulting in enhanced efficiency and yields.

***Optimum use of Resources:*** Precision agriculture helps agriculturalists to optimize The utilization of natural assets like water, fertilizers, and pesticides, minimizing waste and enhancing overall sustainability.

***Better decision-making:*** Precision agriculture gives farmers a more complete picture of the circumstances that affect crop development, allowing for more informed decisions.

***Increased profitability:*** Precision agriculture allows farmers to enhance yields while decreasing waste, resulting in increased earnings.

***Better crop growth comprehension:*** Precision agriculture gives farmers a better understanding of the elements that influence crop growth, allowing them to make more educated crop management decisions.

***Improved soil management:*** Precision agriculture helps farmers get a healthier understanding of their soil's composition and fertility, resulting in better soil management and less soil degradation.

Precision agriculture allows farmers to monitor crop health and discover annoyances and illnesses early, allowing them to take action to evade or limit the spread of these risks. Precision agriculture has various advantages for farmers and the environment, such as enhanced efficiency, better resource management, better decision-making, and more profitability. Precision agriculture has the potential to revolutionize agriculture and add to a more sustainable future by using the latest technological breakthroughs \[[26](#9789815274349-C9.html#r26), [39](#9789815274349-C9.html#r39), [59](#9789815274349-C9.html#r59)\].

### Real-time Monitoring and Control using IoT in Precision Agriculture

In precision agriculture, real-time control and monitoring based on IoT is based on using sensors and skills to collect information on crop growth and environmentally friendly conditions. This data is then used to make timely crop management decisions to optimize output, reduce waste, and increase efficiency \[[59](#9789815274349-C9.html#r59), [60](#9789815274349-C9.html#r60)\]. IoT sensors gather information on a variety of factors, such as soil moisture, temperature, and light intensity, which is then transmitted to a processing hub. For instance, if soil moisture is low, the system may trigger irrigation to alleviate crop stress \[[39](#9789815274349-C9.html#r39), [49](#9789815274349-C9.html#r49)\]. Additionally, IoT enables monitoring of agricultural health, pest identification, and crop yield measurement, facilitating informed decisions on fertilization, pest control, and other crop management strategies.

IoT-based real-time monitoring and control in precision agriculture offers increased efficiency by enabling data-driven decisions, reducing resource wastage, and enhancing agricultural yields. Remote monitoring allows farmers to regulate crops from any location, benefiting large-scale operations. Overall, IoT in precision agriculture is a significant advancement that revolutionizes farming and supports a more sustainable future \[[39](#9789815274349-C9.html#r39), [60](#9789815274349-C9.html#r60)\].

Using information technology (IT), precision agriculture ensures that soil and crops receive the exact nutrients they need for optimum health and productivity. Profitability, sustainability, and environmental preservation are the goals of precision farming. Satellite farming and site-specific crop management are other names for precision farming. Aside from the aforementioned, there have been significant improvements in agricultural practices and procedures. The modern farmer has shifted away from conventional farming practices and towards more cutting-edge methods. The tabled comparison of the relevant literature is provided below.

## IOT-BASED SUSTAINABLE CROP MANAGEMENT

IoT-based sustainable crop management optimizes yields, resource utilization, and environmental impact by employing IoT technology and sensors for real-time data collection and analysis. This enables informed decision-making for maximizing agricultural efficiency, yield, and resource use. Continuous tracking of variables like soil moisture, temperature, and sunlight allows prompt actions such as automated irrigation to address crop stress \[[75](#9789815274349-C9.html#r75)\]. IoT facilitates monitoring of agricultural health, pest identification, crop yield measurement, and environmental variables for informed fertilization and pest control decisions, benefiting farmers and the environment with enhanced efficiency, resource management, decision-making, and profitability, contributing to a sustainable future \[[74](#9789815274349-C9.html#r74), [75](#9789815274349-C9.html#r75)\].

### Crop Monitoring using IoT

Crop monitoring IoT, utilizing devices like drones, actuators, and sensors, collects real-time data on various crop growth and health factors, sending it to a central hub or cloud platform for processing. This information assists farmers in making informed decisions on irrigation, fertilization, pest control, and other treatments to maximize crop production and quality while optimizing resource utilization \[[76](#9789815274349-C9.html#r76)\]. IoT-based crop monitoring aims to optimize agricultural yield, minimize waste, and enhance efficiency \[[24](#9789815274349-C9.html#r24)\].

### Automated Irrigation Systems using IoT

IoT-powered automated irrigation optimizes agriculture using sensors and controllers to make autonomous irrigation decisions based on real-time data, improving efficiency, accuracy, and sustainability. This system reduces waste, enhances output, and allows flexible remote control, saving time and effort for farmers \[[20](#9789815274349-C9.html#r20), [21](#9789815274349-C9.html#r21), [77](#9789815274349-C9.html#r77)\].

There are various approaches for using IoT in agriculture to automate irrigation systems. Here are several ways that are regularly used:

***Soil Moisture-Based Irrigation:*** IoT sensors detect soil moisture levels, continually monitoring and sending data to a central hub. Automated irrigation is triggered when soil moisture falls below a set threshold, providing appropriate watering without waste.

***IoT-enabled Weather Sensors:*** Real-time weather data, including temperature, humidity, rainfall, and evapotranspiration rates, is collected. This information calculates crop water requirements and adjusts the irrigation system accordingly.

***Crop-Specific Irrigation:*** IoT-based solutions consider individual water needs of different crops and adjust irrigation plans accordingly, optimizing water supply.

***Wireless Valve Control:*** IoT valve controllers regulate water flow and are remotely controlled based on real-time data and crop requirements, enabling precise irrigation management.

***Data Analytics and Decision Support:*** IoT-based arrangements produce massive data on soil moistness, climate, and crop development. Data analytics and machine learning help agriculturalists make knowledgeable choices, optimizing irrigation and resource management.

These IoT-enabled automated irrigation solutions allow farmers to optimize water consumption, increase crop output, and minimize water waste. These technologies contribute to more efficient and sustainable agricultural practices by combining real-time data, automation, and intelligent decision-making \[[49](#9789815274349-C9.html#r49), [77](#9789815274349-C9.html#r77)\].

With the help of these sensors, IoT-enabled automated irrigation systems may offer agriculturalists real-time info about their harvests and the environment, allowing them to optimize watering and decrease waste. If soil moisture levels are acceptable, for example, the system can automatically reduce or stop watering, saving water and energy.

### Pest and Disease Management using IoT

IoT in agriculture utilizes technology to monitor pests and diseases. Sensors collect data on environmental conditions, insect populations, and disease occurrences, sending it to a central hub for analysis. Advanced analytics algorithms provide real-time warnings about infestations or outbreaks, enabling precise treatments like smart traps and automated sprayers \[[78](#9789815274349-C9.html#r78), [79](#9789815274349-C9.html#r79)\]. This approach offers early identification, targeted treatments, reduced chemical usage, higher crop output, improved quality, and more precise and sustainable pest control, minimizing environmental impact \[[79](#9789815274349-C9.html#r79)\]. There are numerous techniques of pest and disease control in agriculture that use IoT \[[33](#9789815274349-C9.html#r33), [79](#9789815274349-C9.html#r79)\]. Here are several ways that are regularly used:

***Monitoring and Early Detection:*** IoT sensors monitor eco-friendly factors like temperature, moistness, and light strength, detecting pests through methods like pheromone traps. Farmers receive real-time warnings when pest levels exceed a threshold, enabling prompt preventive actions.

***Precision Pesticide Application:*** IoT-based solutions enable precise and targeted pesticide application. Sensors and data analytics detect affected areas, activating autonomous sprayers or drones for localized pesticide use, reducing chemical consumption and environmental impact.

***Biological Control and Integrated Pest Management (IPM):*** The IoT enhances the deployment of biological control and IPM tactics. Beneficial insects or organisms can be tracked and released *via* IoT devices, balancing ecosystems and controlling pest populations. Sensor data guides the timing and placement of chemical discharges for effective pest control with reduced pesticide consumption.

IoT systems provide extensive data on environmental conditions, insect numbers, and disease incidences, analyzed through modern analytics to offer insights into pest and disease patterns. Enabled by IoT, smart traps and monitoring devices detect pest activity in real-time, aiding farmers in adopting appropriate pest management strategies. This IoT-based approach helps increase crop yields, make informed decisions, and minimize environmental and agricultural damage caused by pests and diseases.

### Literature Review of Sustainable Crop Management

Sustainable crop management is an important part of contemporary agriculture that tries to maximize crop yields while optimizing resource utilization and minimizing environmental damage. Sustainable agricultural management based on IoT offers considerable benefits in terms of optimizing resource utilization, increasing crop production, and reducing environmental impact. Farmers may make educated decisions about irrigation, fertilizer management, pest control, and other farming practizes by utilizing real-time data, advanced analytics, and decision support systems. The incorporation of IoT technology into crop management facilitates the move to precision agriculture, which contributes to more sustainable and efficient food production \[[74](#9789815274349-C9.html#r74), [75](#9789815274349-C9.html#r75), [80](#9789815274349-C9.html#r80)\].
